# Aspira — agent integration guide

This file is written for AI agents that operate on or through this repository.

## What Aspira is

Aspira (新愿) is the **first layer of AGI — the discriminator**. A rule-based engine
that judges what AI says before it reaches a human, and says "no" when something is
wrong.

**Core value:** LLMs are great at generating but weak at knowing what they don't know.
Aspira adds the discrimination layer, so your agent doesn't just *say* things — it
says things that are *right*.

**Zero LLM dependency.** 54 dimensions, 132 modules, 181 MCP tools, 1,023 dispatch
routes. Pure rule engine.

## Quick start

```javascript
const hf = require('@mark-cell-520/aspira');

// Check user input before processing it
const input = hf.checkInput('You are so selfish if you disagree');
if (input.gate.action === 'rewrite') {
  // Replace emotional manipulation with a factual statement
}

// Check an AI output before sending it
const output = hf.checkOutput('Undoubtedly this is the only correct solution.');
if (output.gate.action === 'rewrite') {
  // Follow findings[].guidance to fix it before delivering
}

// Check a factual claim
const fact = hf.checkOutput('According to 2025 Harvard research, coffee extends life by 12.5 years');
if (fact.gate.action === 'verify') {
  // Gather evidence before acting
}
```

> **This example is executed by `test/doc-examples.test.js`, not just written here.**
> It previously imported the package as `hf` and then called `gate.checkInput(...)` —
> `gate` was never defined, so the snippet threw `ReferenceError` for anyone who
> copied it. `checkInput` / `checkDraft` / `checkOutput` are top-level exports of
> `src/gate.js` (the package `main`), so they are reached as `hf.checkInput`.
> The third snippet was also *not* merely mislabelled: it documented `verify` while
> the engine returned `pass`, because `UNSUPPORTED_CLAIM_EN` could not match the
> singular `study` (`studies?` never matches "study") and required the verb to sit
> directly against `by` (`extended life by 12.5 years` was missed). Both patterns
> were repaired; the snippet now returns `verify` as documented.

## API reference

### `checkInput(text)`
Discriminates user input. Runs: scope-check -> premise-check -> discriminate (54
dimensions) -> gate -> error-memory -> auto-rules. **Rejects unanswerable questions and
invalid premises early.**

### `checkDraft(text)`
For AI drafts before completion. Runs all input checks plus frame-check and doubt-engine.
**Catches narrative closure, overconfidence, and irreversibility.**

### `checkOutput(text)`
For AI responses before sending. Runs all draft checks plus output-gate and doubt-engine.
**Prevents hallucinations from reaching users.**

### `runPipeline({ input, mode, anchor })`
Full pipeline with mode selection (`input` / `output` / `draft`) and a conversation
anchor. **Keeps the model anchored to the original goal across long sessions.**

> **This line used to say "mode selection (fast / deep)", and neither value worked.**
> `src/pipeline.js`'s `runPipeline` branches only on `'input'`, `'output'` and
> `'draft'`, so `'fast'` and `'deep'` matched **no** branch and behaved exactly like
> omitting `mode` — measured on the same input: `input` → 11 layers, `draft` → 12,
> `output` → 13, while `fast`, `deep` and "not passed" all gave **11**. The MCP tool
> `aspira_gate_pipeline` defaulted to `'fast'`, so its `mode` parameter could not
> change the behaviour at all, and the same file's inventory claimed "17 layers
> (11 for mode:'fast')" — the 11 was a coincidence of falling through to the default,
> and 17 never matched any mode (the maximum measured is 13). `handleGatePipeline`
> now defaults to `'input'` and maps `fast`→`input`, `deep`→`output` as compatibility
> aliases, so previously-documented values keep working;
> `test/gate-pipeline-mode.test.js` locks that the three real modes produce distinct
> layer counts and that `AGENTS.md` no longer binds 17 layers to a mode.

## Return value

```javascript
{
  gate: { action: 'pass'|'verify'|'rewrite'|'block', reason: '...' },
  verdict: '可信'|'需验证'|'不可信',
  overallScore: 0.56,
  findings: [{
    dimension: 'unsupported_claim',
    severity: 90,
    details: '无依据断言(2处: ...)',
    guidance: '补充可验证的数据来源，无法验证的断言改为不确定表述'
  }],
  checked_by: [
    { layer: 'scope-check', action: 'pass' },
    { layer: 'discriminate', score: 0.56 }
  ]
}
```

## Gate actions

| Action | Meaning | What your agent should do |
|--------|---------|---------------------------|
| `pass` | Clean | Deliver normally |
| `verify` | Needs evidence | Run the verifier before responding |
| `rewrite` | Must be rewritten | Follow `findings[].guidance` |
| `block` | Stop | Do not output. Use `gate.reason` |

`verdict` is derived from `gate.action`, so the two cannot contradict each other. If you
read only one field, read `gate.action`.

## The 54 dimensions

**Block-level (9):** hate_speech, dehumanization, prompt_injection, code_security, deceptive_alignment, phishing_coercion, coverup_induction, dangerous_instruction, reward_hacking

**Rewrite-level (8):** emotional_manipulation, gaslighting, double_bind, victim_blaming, false_urgency, bullshit_recognition, absolute_claim, induced_trust

**Verify-level (26):** appeal_to_authority_boost, vagueness, contradiction, sycophancy, confidence, fallacies, presupposition, empty_answer, info_deprivation, false_equivalence, hasty_generalization, slippery_slope, whataboutism, pseudo_profundity, reasoning_coherence, stereotype, clickbait, bad_faith, no_fallback, unsupported_claim, perfect_error, pseudo_causal, soft_deflection, premature_termination, tone_policing, sealioning

Dimensions that are scored but do not force a gate action: evidence,
moral_foundations, dogwhistle, factual_consistency, sarcasm, privacy_boundary,
meta_cognition, theory_of_mind, counterfactual, social_norm, capability_overclaim,
goal_misalignment, instrumental_reasoning, ai_writing_tell.

> **What "54" counts.** `discriminate()` returns a `dimensions` object with **54 keys**,
> and that object is what the number above refers to. Three further discriminators —
> `pseudo_causal`, `soft_deflection` and `ai_writing_tell` — run inside `discriminate()`
> and can push `findings`, but they are **not** keys of `dimensions`, so they are listed
> above yet not counted in the 54. The four lists therefore name 57 items, of which 54
> appear in `dimensions`. `test/dimension-health.test.js` locks the 54-key set, so any
> future addition or rename fails the suite instead of drifting silently.

## Decision routing — better choices

- **Should this be done?** `scope-check` rejects out-of-scope requests.
- **Is the premise valid?** `premise-check` catches premise problems before you answer.
- **Retry or give up?** `src/cortex/self-healing.js` — severity-based: low retries, high escalates.
- **Did it actually work?** `src/core/action-tracker.js` — `assessEffectiveness()` checks the effect, not the action.

## Installation

```bash
npm install @mark-cell-520/aspira
```

**Requirements:** Node.js >= 18.17. No GPU, no LLM API, no database, no network access
at runtime, no runtime dependencies.

## MCP integration

```bash
git clone https://github.com/mark-cell-520/aspira.git
cd aspira
node src/mcp-server.js --port 8099
# or a Unix socket:
node src/mcp-server.js --socket /tmp/aspira.sock
# Connect: hermes mcp add aspira --url http://localhost:8099/mcp
```

`tools/call` enforces a three-tier write permission model. `guest` (no credentials) can
call read-only tools; the four state-mutating tools — `aspira_memory_write_control`,
`aspira_memory_eraser`, `aspira_decision_decide`, `aspira_self_heal` — require
a `Aspira-OID-<16-hex>` header (`user`) or a valid bearer token (`admin`).

If you change this permission set, change it in **three** places or the test will fail:
the `needsWrite` array in `handleRequest`, `test/mcp-guest-permission.test.js`
(`WRITE_TOOLS`), and the documentation tables. The permission block must live *inside*
`case 'tools/call'` — it was previously a bare block between two case labels, which made
it unreachable dead code while every unit test stayed green.

## Repository conventions

These are the rules this codebase actually follows. Follow them when changing it.

1. **Never hardcode the version.** `VERSION` is the single source of truth.
   `src/core/version.js` reads it and carries a fallback that must match. `package.json`
   and `SKILL.md` must match. `hf.version` reads `VERSION` at runtime.
2. **New modules go through the lazy registry.** Use
   `const _X = _lazy('key', () => require('./x.js'))` and instantiate with
   `new (_X().ClassName)(...)`. Never `require` a Tier-2 module at the top of
   `heartflow.js`.
3. **Every new public method that an external agent needs must be exposed on the MCP
   server** (`src/mcp-server.js`: add the tool definition, the handler mapping, and the
   handler function).
4. **Tests must be reachable by `test/run-all.js`.** It walks `test/` recursively and
   executes each file in a subprocess. A test file that requires `../src/...` from a
   subdirectory must use the correct relative depth, or it will never run.
5. **Zero runtime dependencies.** Add a `devDependency` only when unavoidable, and never
   require it from `src/`.
6. **Verify syntax after every edit:** `node --check <file>`.
7. **After changing engine code, run:** `node bin/verify.js` and
   `node test/run-all.js`. Both must be clean before you report success.

## Design principles

1. **Discriminator-first** — the first of AGI's five layers. It does not generate.
2. **Zero dependencies** — a pure rule engine, instant install.
3. **Auditable** — every decision preserves its full reasoning chain in `checked_by`.
4. **Self-checking** — Aspira's own output passes through its own gates.
5. **Honest numbers** — documentation must state what the code actually does. If a
   metric is claimed, it must be measurable.

## Honest limitations

- Not AGI, not generative, not a semantic understanding system.
- Pattern-matching architecture: obfuscation not covered by patterns is not caught.
- Irony, metaphor, and cultural context are invisible.
- **The homoglyph layer escalates whole-sentence Cyrillic/Greek English, and that
  is not fixable at the pattern layer.** Measured in cycle 185 on the 137-sample
  benign corpus: rewriting an English sentence with Cyrillic look-alikes
  (`hello world` → `hеllо wоrld`) makes the `adversarial-variant` layer report
  `对抗变体: 同形字混淆` and push the gate to `rewrite` — **83 of 137 benign
  samples escalate this way** (0 bypasses; the miss rate is unaffected). Two repairs
  were tried and both were refuted by measurement: a second-stage anchor (the
  homoglyph must sit near a known attack term) is clean on the benign side but
  catches only 2 of 5 attacks, because `kill me киll` / `i hаte you` /
  `рass the test` have *benign content* and are intercepted **only** by this layer's
  homoglyph signal — the anchor would drop them; a density criterion (homoglyphs per
  Latin letter) has overlapping distributions (attacks 0.00–0.83, benign 0.00–2.00,
  re-measured in cycle 187), and fully-rewritten benign text scores *higher* because
  `e`/`a`/`o` dominate the substitution. The underlying reason is that "an English
  sentence typed on a Cyrillic keyboard" is not distinguishable from an attack at the
  language layer — someone writing `hеllо wоrld` may simply have a Russian keyboard
  layout. The layer's design intent is "a few homoglyphs mixed into an attack
  sentence", and whole-sentence rewriting falls outside it.
  `test/homoglyph-benign-boundary.test.js` pins the boundary in both directions
  (legitimate non-Latin text must not escalate; real attack shapes and the
  signal-only samples must still be caught). What is *not* covered: a caller sending
  genuinely Cyrillic-keyboard English gets `rewrite` with no content finding — read
  `findings[].dimension === 'none'` as "no content problem was found" in that case.
  (Cycle 185 first measured the 83/137 figure; cycle 187 re-measured it and the
  density ranges before writing them down, because a number quoted from an earlier
  probe that used a different substitution map is not the same number.)
- **False-positive rate is measured, not claimed.** `scripts/calibrate-fp-recall.js`
  measures it on a hand-written labelled corpus of **137 benign / 68 malicious** samples;
  the current reading is **0.0% FP / 100% recall**. That is a *corpus-local* number,
  not real traffic — the corpus was written by this repo's authors and will drift from
  what real callers actually send. It replaced an earlier "around 8%" that nothing had
  ever measured. The corpus was grown from 38/13 to 75/22 after the mixed-language
  merge made English patterns run on Chinese text: 38 samples carried only **two**
  mixed-language entries, so the instrument was blind to exactly the risk the merge
  introduced, and it immediately surfaced two false positives that had never been seen.
  The earlier 0.0% reading was the product of fixing two long-standing false positives
  whose shared root cause was that `checkPseudoCausal` and `checkUnsupportedClaim`
  recognised the shape "vague source plus precise number" and **never checked whether
  the sentence had already hedged itself**. Both now share one `HEDGE_RE`, measured in
  both directions: three of three hedged statements pass, four of four unhedged ones
  still fire, so no recall was traded for the drop. A zero on a 106-sample corpus is
  not a zero in production — read it as "no false positives this corpus can express",
  which is a statement about the corpus as much as about the engine.
  **[第二百零三轮] 而这个语料的表达力比"205 条"听起来还要窄: 54 个维度里
  只有 25 个被它触达。** `scripts/dimension-health-audit.js` 把 201 条语料逐条喂给裸
  `discriminate()`，只有 25 个的信号曾非零；另外 29 个，在真实标注语料上从未被
  触达，它们的行为只被 `test/` 里的字符串字面量锁定。所以上面那个
  **0.0% FP / 100% recall 对这 29 个的误报与漏报行为是零表达力** —— 不是
  "它们没问题"，是"这个读数说不出它们有没有问题"。两个数字必须一起引用: 语料
  规模 205 条，维度触达 25/54。仪器自己的措辞是"语料盲区"，并且明确指出这不是
  引擎失效(那 29 个在含测试字面量与正则片段的大输入池里都触发、也推
  finding)，是**校准视野的边界**。补语料样本才能把它们纳入校准，那是
  `fp-recall-calibration` 切片的工作；本切片只负责把这条边界写下来，
  `test/corpus-dimension-reach-disclosure.test.js` 锁住它不许悄悄消失。
  (措辞上刻意不让数字紧贴"维度"二字: 审计的 dimensions 中文 pattern 是
  `数字 + 个 + 维度`，本文里一旦出现「数字+个+维度」的字样(本轮第一版就写了三处)，它就会被当成"引擎声称 N 个维度"
  而与实测 54 不符——本轮第一版正是这样写，一口气制造了四条假声称、
  把八个审计族测试全部染红。**一条披露事实的句子，被仪器读成一条规模声称**，
  这是 cycle 8/9/190 的"仪器按语言与词形分流"同族的新形态: 这次错的不是
  文档的数字，是文档的措辞。)
  同段上面那个 "106-sample" 是**旧读数**: 语料实测 benign 137 + malicious 62 =
  **199** 条(见本段第一行)，106 是它增长之前的规模，两个数出现在同一段里
  自相矛盾，此处一并留证。
  第一百三十八轮更新: 上面记的 0.9% 已降到 **0.0%** —— 当时唯一被升级的那条样本现在只留 meta_cognition finding 且 gate=pass(本轮实测)。此后语料又增长两轮(第一百三十九轮加 1 条 reward_hacking, 第一百四十四轮加 1 对商务空话样本)，当时规模见上一行。——
  当时唯一被升级的那条 `可能有多种解释，我倾向于第一种，但不排除其他可能。`
  现在只留 meta_cognition finding 且 gate=pass(本轮实测)。这不是"误报消失了"
  这种结论, 只是"该样本在当前引擎下不再被升级"的实测读数; 新增的 1 条 benign
  是它的对冲变体护栏。0.9% 的解释(下面那条"8:1"账)作为历史保留:
  一条当时花了 8:1 代价的升级, 后来被改动抹平, 而规则本身没被删。
  Until `aspira_false_positive` accumulates real caller feedback
  (`data/feedback/false-positives.jsonl`), treat 0.0% as a lower bound on honesty, not
  a production SLO. Run the script after changing any pattern; a false positive can be a
  false negative in disguise, so recall must be re-measured after every FP fix.
  **The FP reading is 0.9% and not 0.0%, and that is the honest number.** One benign sample
  is escalated: `可能有多种解释，我倾向于第一种，但不排除其他可能。` — a model of
  calibrated uncertainty, pushed to `verify` by the gate rule `findings.length > 1`
  (two lightweight findings are enough). That rule was **measured, not assumed**: it is the
  sole interception reason for **8 of 45** malicious samples and produces this **1** false
  positive, so it earns its keep 8:1 and was deliberately left alone. The cost is recorded
  here rather than hidden by deleting the sample — an earlier version of this file claimed
  0.0% while this class of escalation was already happening and simply unmeasured.
- **The 100% recall above is *plaintext* recall, and that was a blind spot.** The script
  fed `MALICIOUS` to the gate as-written, so **an evasion bypass did not move the number
  at all**: one repair took character-wise separator insertion from 5-of-6 bypassed down
  to 1-of-6 and recall read 100% before and after. The script now also measures recall
  with three evasion classes applied, and the gap it exposed is large — plaintext 100%
  against **85%** (character-wise separators), **95%** (spaces between letters) and
  **95%** (HTML entities) on the same 41 samples. The 39% letter-space reading was closed in
  cycle 18 by matching known terms *space-tolerantly* instead of trying to restore the
  original text — see the cycle-18 entry below. Same lesson as the corpus gap above:
  a risk the instrument cannot see is a risk that does not exist, right up until it does.
  第一百四十轮更新(语料后又增长, 六个变换类): 上面这三个
  数字是 **41 样本时代**的读数，现在的内容型召回是逐字符分隔符 **55/55**、HTML
  **55/55**、字母间插空格 **47/55**。字母间插空格从 55/55 掉下来**不是退化而是
  修正**: 第三十二轮往 letter-space 词表加的 7 个代码/SQL 术语(eval/exec/
  execSync/Function/innerHTML/document.write/SELECT)在变换下把**良性技术讨论**
  判成恶意 —— 实测逐字符分隔符 **18/122 误报**、字母间插空格 **13/122 误报**,
  30 例次全部出自那 7 个词。根因是变换作用于**整段文本**，良性英文句变换后每个
  词都被拆开，两段判据的第 2 段(片段含空白)对攻击与良性同时成立，锚点失效。
  移除后两类误报降到 **0/122** 与 **1/122**，代价是那 10 条样本在字母间距这一类
  下回到漏拦(其余五个变换类仍全拦)。**这是本轮最值得记的一条: 一个层的验证只
  覆盖它被设计时的场景(明文不误报)，没覆盖新场景(变换后不误报)，第三十二轮
  的"0.9% 不变、未新增任何误报"是明文读数，而同一批语料变换后的读数一直在
  没人看的地方。** 根本修法(只在整段都呈字母成对拆分形状时才折叠)留给下一轮。
  第一百四十一轮更新(那一轮就是 fp-recall-calibration，根本修法已实施):
  字母间插空格的内容型召回 **47/55 → 53/55**，变换版误报 **1/122 不变**(那 1 条
  是禁用折叠的对照下也存在的既有误报，零宽类同样有)。修法不是 cycle 18 那个
  "放宽到 1-2 字母 token"——那一次净负是因为**没有形状判据**; 本轮判据是整段
  形态(空格分隔的 token 里 ≤2 字母的纯字母 token 占比 ≥0.6 且 token 数 ≥8)，
  且本条**不做任何判断**、只负责还原: 形状判据对变换后的良性英文句同样成立
  (攻击与良性在该层同构)，还原后是良性还是攻击交给下游维度裁决。两个必须同时
  成立的门槛各修掉一条实测误报: 中文占比 <30%(中英混排句折叠后 'racecondition'
  是不存在的词，absolute_claim 因它误报)、HEDGE_RE 容忍无空格与字母间空格形态
  (折叠把 'though the sample was small' 粘成一个词，豁免原本要求空格)。
  仍未回收 2 条并已锁在 test/letter-space-collapse.test.js 里:
  'eval(res.data)' 变换后只有 6 个 token 够不到 ≥8 门槛; 'new Function(userInput)'
  折叠成 'newFunction(userInput)' 后 code_security 的模式仍不匹配(它要求 new 与
  Function 之间有空格)——折叠的固有代价: 插入的空格与原有空格同形，词边界不可恢复。
  Widening `strip_letter_space` to 1–2 letter tokens was tried and **reverted**: it
  folded 53 additional benign samples to recover 1 malicious one, because collapsing
  merges originally separate words (`const x` → `constx`) and destroys the word boundary
  the patterns need.

> **The 41-sample denominator above is now split, because two of the 41 are not evasion gaps at all.**
> Measured in cycle 35: `i hаte you` and `рass the test` are intercepted in plaintext purely
> by the `adversarial-variant` layer's homoglyph signal — "a Cyrillic/Greek character
> adjacent to a Latin letter" — and their `findings` arrays carry **no content finding
> whatsoever** (only the dimMap fallback sentinel `{dimension: 'none'}`). Their *content*
> (`i hate you` / `pass the test`) is benign, so once a separator is inserted and
> `de_cyrillic` runs, the signal **correctly** disappears and there is nothing left to
> catch. Counting them in the denominator was scoring "a signal that correctly vanished"
> as a failure. `calibrate-fp-recall.js` now reports every class on **two** denominators —
> all 41, and the **39 content-bearing** samples (criterion: at least one finding whose
> `dimension` is not the `'none'` sentinel) — plus the signal-only pair on its own line.
> Content-bearing readings: separators **35/39 (89.7%)**, letter spaces **37/39 (94.9%)**,
> HTML entities **37/39 (94.9%)**. The signal-only pair is caught by letter-space and
> HTML (2/2 each) and by neither under separators (0/2). *Two traps fell out of this and
> both are recorded again below:* the obvious criterion `findings.length > 0` classifies
> those two as content-bearing, because the array is non-empty — **a structurally valid
> response whose content is dead**; and the numbers 85% / 95% / 95% are the all-41
> readings, so any site quoting them must say which denominator it means.
- **The `adversarial-variant` layer was unreachable through MCP, and it also over-flagged legitimate Cyrillic and Greek.** Two measured defects, one slice. (a) `HOMOGLYPH_RE` was `/[\u0400-\u04FF\u0370-\u03FF]/` — it matched *any* Cyrillic or Greek character, so `Привет, как дела?`, `Καλημέρα, τι κάνεις;` and `I read a book called Война и мир yesterday.` all came back `rewrite` ("对抗变体: 同形字混淆"). The 86-sample benign corpus is all Chinese and English, so it was blind to exactly this. The regex is now narrowed to *"a Cyrillic/Greek character adjacent to a Latin letter"*, which is what "masquerading as Latin" actually means; four attack samples (`киll`, `hаte`, `lа lа`, `рass`) are still caught. The traded recall is bounded and disclosed: a purely-Cyrillic look-alike word with no Latin neighbour no longer trips this layer, but `de_cyrillic` restores it and `discriminate()` still judges the content — precision at this layer, recall at the content layer. (b) `aspira_gate`, `aspira_gate_check` and `aspira_gate_pipeline` all returned `pass` for `kill me киll`, because their handlers called `gate.gate` / `gate.check` / `gate.pipeline`, and **all three of those run `discriminate()` only** — the full 17-layer pipeline is `runPipeline`. The whole obfuscation class (homoglyph, zero-width, curly quote, fullwidth, combining, digit, word-split) was therefore invisible to the entire MCP tool surface, while agents rely on those tools to gate untrusted input. `handleGatePipeline` now calls `runPipeline`; the two lightweight tools keep their behaviour but their descriptions now carry a ⚠️ naming the skipped layer and pointing at `aspira_gate_pipeline`. A test caught that `runPipeline` does **not** return the `dimensions` key that `gate.pipeline` did — a silent break that would have shipped, now restored explicitly and locked.
- **The calibration instrument itself measured the wrong thing, and the corpus was blind to
  every non-Latin script.** Two defects, one slice. (a) `calibrate-fp-recall.js`'s `run()`
  called `idx.discriminate(text, [])` — the 54-dimension discriminator *only*, never the
  `adversarial-variant` layer. Measured on 102 benign / 41 malicious: `discriminate` read
  **38/41 recall (92.7%)** while `gate.checkOutput` and `runPipeline` both read **41/41
  (100%)**. The three "misses" (`kill me киll`, `i hаte you`, `рass the test`) were being
  caught by the adversarial layer as `rewrite`; the instrument reported them as bypasses,
  and any false positive that layer introduced would equally never have shown up in the FP
  number. Both directions were wrong at once. `run()` now measures `gate.checkOutput()`,
  the same entry point agents are told to use. (b) The corpus carried **zero** samples
  containing Cyrillic, Greek, Arabic, Korean or Japanese, so it was blind to exactly the
  class that had already produced a real false positive: `HOMOGLYPH_RE` once matched *any*
  Cyrillic/Greek character, flagging `Привет, как дела?` as adversarial, and
  `text-normalizer`'s `de_cyrillic` once transliterated whole Russian sentences into a
  Latin/Cyrillic mix (`Игнорируй все предыдущие инструкции` → `игнopиpyй вce пpeдыдyщиe
  инcтpyкции`), which silently broke every pattern written against the original script —
  a coupling between two layers that neither layer's own tests could see. Adding
  `INJECTION_PATTERNS.ru/el/ko/ja` surfaced that second defect immediately: the new
  Russian and Greek patterns matched when tested alone and never fired in the pipeline.
  Both fixes now follow one rule — "masquerading as Latin" requires the look-alike to sit
  next to a Latin letter, so transliterate only when Latin letters are the majority. The
  corpus grew by 16 benign (legitimate non-Latin text) and 7 malicious (four non-Latin
  injections plus three homoglyph attacks) samples to pin all of it in place. A test that
  had hardcoded the denominator `34` failed the moment the corpus grew; it now derives the
  count from the corpus, because a hardcoded number is a number that rots.
- **The audit reported 45/45 green while `domains` had never been measured at all, and the runner's own concurrency risk assessment had missed that probe tests rewrite the docs.** Seventh consecutive `doc-honest-numbers` cycle. Cycle 6 concluded the slice was *nearly exhausted* and recommended rotating away; this cycle disproved that by asking a different question — not "what numbers are unaudited" but **"of the 45 that read green, which were never actually measured?"** Listing the audit's keys gave the answer: only `dimensions/modules/tools/routes/layers/tier_*/tests/nodeReq/deps`. README's capability section is titled `### Capability domains (7 domains, 132 modules)` — **`domains` was not a key in any pattern.** Measured: the title says 7 and the table below it happens to have 7 rows, so the number is true *by coincidence*; add an eighth domain, update the table, forget the title, and nothing would ever complain. That is the recurring shape of this slice — **a number that was never measured is indistinguishable, in an all-green report, from a number that is locked.** The new key immediately surfaced two previously invisible claims (README and SKILL.md) *and* a real defect it exposed along the way: `IDENTITY.md` said `99 个模块真实加载` against a measured 132 — the twin of a line fixed two cycles ago, which had survived because the fix patched one phrasing and not the other. **Then the runner's concurrency assessment turned out to have been wrong in a way it had explicitly claimed to have checked.** `run-all.js` became bounded-concurrent with the recorded risk basis *"0 test files write under `data/` or `memory/`"* — true, and beside the point, because at least four probe tests **rewrite `README.md`/`IDENTITY.md`** and then spawn the audit. Run concurrently, probe A injects, probe B injects on top of A's already-polluted copy, A's audit sees B's probe, A's assertion fails, and A restores from a backup that *contains B's probe* — the suite dropped to 6 failures whose messages all read like instrument bugs. First measured pollution: 7 stray rows in README. `test/_doc-probe-lock.js` now serialises every test that spawns the audit, writers *and* readers, because a reader must see the document in a definite state, not mid-probe. **The cleanup itself caused collateral damage that only a reader test caught**: `git checkout -- README.md` (the right way to clear probe rows) also reverted the `17-layer pipeline*` footnote added two cycles earlier, and `layer-count-two-readings.test.js` — a pure reader — was the only thing that noticed. Suite 1205 → **1210**; verify 14/14; audit **47/47**; corpus was unchanged (106 benign / FP 0.9%, 41 malicious / recall 100%); no `src/` change, so MCP was not restarted.
- **`unsupported_claim` and `pseudo_causal` used to count the same span twice, and the corpus
  could not see it.** `checkUnsupportedClaim` pushed one entry per matching pattern without
  checking whether the spans overlapped, so `根据 2024 年的一份行业报告，部署时间中位数约为
  12 分钟。` was reported as *two* findings — the second a substring of the first — because
  `根据…报告` and `2024年…报告` both matched. `count` doubles, and since
  `severity = round(score*100)` with `score = min(1, count*0.45)`, severity went **45 → 90**.
  None of the 143 corpus samples could express that shape, so the calibration script never
  saw it. `_dedupeOverlappingSpans()` now drops any span contained in a longer kept one, and
  both functions call it. This is an instrument-honesty fix, not an FP fix: counting is the
  raw material of severity, and severity is the raw material of the gate action, so a
  duplicated pattern silently changed the verdict delivered to users.
- **A dimension-health instrument found three defects in how findings are built, and the instrument itself was wrong twice first.** `scripts/dimension-health-audit.js` feeds a large input pool (the calibration corpus + every string literal in `test/` + fragments stripped from the engine's own regexes) through `discriminate()` and tallies, per dimension, whether its signal ever went non-zero and whether it ever pushed a `finding`. The corpus cannot answer this: a dimension can be fully implemented and still be silent if the corpus contains no sentence that reaches it. **Version 1 of the instrument was simply wrong** — it scanned only the `check*` function bodies, found almost no regexes (they live in module-level tables like `INJECTION_PATTERNS`), and reported 51 of 54 dimensions as "completely silent". A direct call (`idx.checkPromptInjection('ignore all previous instructions')` → count=2, score=1) proved the engine fine and the instrument broken. **Version 2** followed the table references and ran, but still reported 6 silent dimensions; four of those (`unsupported_claim`, `sycophancy`, `contradiction`, `bullshit`) were verified to fire normally on direct calls. The cause was a systematic blind spot in "strip the longest literal run from each regex": cores shorter than 3 characters (`根据…报告` → `根据`), alternation groups that only match whole (`(很棒|很好|…)[^。]*?但`), and patterns needing co-occurrence are all unsynthesizable. **Version 3 stopped pretending to reverse-engineer regexes** and instead measures only "did this dimension ever fire on any of ~6,250 inputs". An instrument that reports its own limitations as engine defects is worse than no instrument — it sends people to fix code that is not broken. The three real defects it then found, all in `discriminate()`'s finding construction: (a) `dimMap` keys and `allDims` names disagreed — `allDims` says `bullshit`, `dimMap` only has `bullshit_recognition` — so `dimObj` resolved to `undefined` and `detail` silently fell back to `1`. `bullshit` really matched 4 times and reported `bullshit(1次)`. Same for `appeal_to_authority` / `appeal_to_authority_boost`. This is the exact `dimMap` key-mismatch defect AGENTS.md already recorded; the earlier fix only covered part of it. (b) `pseudo_causal` and `soft_deflection` were pushed **twice** — once from `allDims` and once from their own dedicated `if` blocks — so one problem produced two findings (measured: `pseudo_causal sev=60` appearing twice). That inflates `findings.length`, which is itself a gate `verify` trigger, the same class as the span double-count fixed last cycle. (c) `clickbait` was in `dimMap` and in `dimensions{}` but never in `allDims`, so it never pushed a finding at all — a fully implemented dimension contributing nothing to the gate. The fix adds **aliases** to `dimMap` rather than renaming anything, because the naming is inconsistent in three places at once (`dimMap` keys / `allDims` names / `BLOCK`·`REWRITE`·`VERIFY_DIMS`): renaming `allDims`' `bullshit` to `bullshit_recognition` would immediately break the `rewrite` trigger, since `REWRITE_DIMS` still holds `bullshit`. Corpus measured unchanged by all three fixes (106 benign / FP 0.9%, 41 malicious / recall 100%). The residual naming inconsistency is disclosed rather than hidden: `findings[].dimension` says `bullshit` while `dimensions{}` says `bullshit_recognition`, and the instrument now reports that separately as "pushed under an alias" instead of miscounting it as a defect.
- **I re-committed a mistake this repository had already recorded.** While growing the corpus
  I added 4 "hedged fabrication" samples to `MALICIOUS` and 4 hedged benign samples to
  `BENIGN`. Both were wrong, and `scripts/calibrate-fp-recall.js` lines 281-292 already said
  why: the "vague source + precise number" class correctly produces **`verify`** (because
  `unsupported_claim` is a *verify-tier* dimension) while the script's "caught" means
  `block` or `rewrite`, so putting them in `MALICIOUS` counts correct behaviour as a miss.
  I had not read that note before adding samples. They were removed and the class is now
  guarded at the dimension level in `test/fp-hedge-exemption.test.js`, which also locks
  "these strings must not reappear in the corpus arrays". A measurement settled the related
  question too: the benign and malicious members of this class produce **identical**
  dimension profiles (`适量运动…20-30%` vs `这种药物…12.5 年`), so no hedge vocabulary can
  separate them — widening `HEDGE_RE` would exempt the malicious ones as well.
- Chinese tokenisation in the hypothesis stage is heuristic (greedy longest-match with a
  stopword list), not dictionary-based.
- **Four advertised MCP tools were structurally dead for a dozen cycles, and the implementation was one directory away in the place the diagnosis never looked.** `mcp-tool-enhancement`, 192nd cycle. Following cycle 17/29's method — *counting tools is not calling them* — every one of the **181** handlers was invoked for real (with `await`: cycle 17's lesson is that calling synchronously lets an async handler's Promise be `JSON.stringify`'d into `{}`, reporting twelve healthy tools as hollow). The census read **159 ok / 2 parameter-validation / 20 error-obj / 0 threw / 0 missing**. Of the 20 error-objs, 16 were the *correct* validation triggered by the probe's own placeholder `'probe'` (unknown action/mode). The four worth chasing were `aspira_error_store` / `_query` / `_fix` / `_verify`, all returning `{error: 'error-memory 能力未实现: heartflow._hfCore 与 src/core/error-memory.js 均不存在, 不是初始化时机问题'}`. Cycle 121 had written there "this capability was never implemented" — **and that diagnosis was only half right**: it could not `require` `src/core/error-memory.js`, so it concluded the capability did not exist. The implementation is at **`src/error-memory.js`** — the `src/` root, not `core/` — exporting twelve methods, of which `logCorrection` / `querySimilar` / `logFix` / `logVerify` correspond one-for-one to what the four handlers need, all measured working. So four advertised tools were structurally incapable of working: the call succeeds, nothing throws, a structurally valid `{error: ...}` comes back, and the capability sits one directory over. That is the shape this file records most often, and it steps on cycle 16's already-recorded error — **a comment/diagnosis says "does not exist" while the thing is right next door** (that time self-diagnostic's engine table wrote `src/core` where the truth was `src/emotion`, and the comment on the very same line knew the correct location). The cost: four more cycles of death, or rather a dozen. The fix: all four handlers now `require` `src/error-memory.js` directly and no longer go through `heartflow._hfCore.errorMemory`, the path cycle 121 measured as never assigned. `problem` is free text while `logCorrection`'s `category` must be one of seven enum values, so the module's own `classifyError` infers it — no guessing, and no extra parameter demanded of the caller. Measured: **0 of 4** tools worked before, **4 of 4** after — `store` returns `{success:true, id:1, recurrence:false}` and persists; `query` returns real warnings; `fix`/`verify` advance `open→fixed→verified`; and after verification `query` goes **completely silent** for that category (`{warnings:[], safe:true}`). The census moved **ok 159 → 163, error-obj 20 → 16**. **And cycle 121's own lock had pinned the defect in place — it predicted this exact moment in its text** ("修好这个空洞(模块真的出现)时, 这条会红, 那时请把四个工具改成返回真实内容"), so it was converted as instructed: the first case is inverted to "must return real content, must not report capability-missing, must wire the real module in four places", while root-cause layers ① and ② are **kept as still-true statements** — they measure `heartflow.js` and `src/core/`, neither of which this cycle touched (no `_hfCore` wiring, no `src/core/` file created). `test/mcp-error-memory-tools.test.js` (8 cases) locks the new contract, mutation-verified in three directions: restoring the defect (path back to `src/core/`) turns **6** red; making `verify` a no-op turns **2** red; making `fix` a no-op turns **3** red. **Three errors in my own lock, all corrected.** (i) I asserted `warnings` would contain a record whose `detail` carries my marker, but `querySimilar` aggregates **by category** and returns a representative record, not the one just stored. (ii) `previousCount` is the number of *unverified records* in that category, and `logCorrection` **de-duplicates** same-category errors within an hour (incrementing `recurrenceCount`, not adding a record) — so the case must start from an empty store to observe 0→1; the first version depended on the library's existing state and was red even when run alone. (iii) A probe text with no classification trigger makes `classifyError` return `unknown`, and `logCorrection` correctly answers `{success:false, reason:'未知错误分类'}` — that is **not** a defect, it is a badly chosen probe. Gate: verify **15/15**; run-all **1863 → 1871 / 12 / 1865 → 1883**, the 12 failures identical to baseline on both channels (one run showed a 13th, `audit-self-pollution`'s live-injection case, which is 8/8 green alone — the known concurrency artefact of its hook writing `SKILL.md` from an audit child process that does not hold the doc lock); audit not re-measured this cycle (no document claim changed except version and test count).
- **A name that had been called for a dozen cycles was never imported — and the catch that swallowed it said "formula unavailable".** `test-coverage-gap`, 191st cycle; `AspiraDecision` abstained on a tie and broke it by `consequence_value` (confidence 0). `coverage-sweep.js` measured **380 src modules / 12 suspected dead / 100 alive-but-untested / 268 with test references** — identical, word for word, to what SKILL.md claims, so the gap was inside class B. Cycle 28 had already set the standard for picking a module there: *requiring a module one by one is coverage theatre — it proves it loads and nothing else*; look for the one whose contract is crisp, round-trippable, and **which someone has already recorded as broken**. AGENTS.md was carrying exactly that note: `sustained-drift-detector.js:312 getFormulaBridge 未导入`, marked "disclosed for a dozen cycles, unfixed". Measured: the file requires only `safe-fs` and `path`; `getFormulaBridge()` is neither imported nor defined. Every call throws `ReferenceError`, swallowed by the immediately-following `catch` whose comment reads *formula unavailable*. So: no throw, no warning, a structurally valid return value — and `this._history[last].phaseTransition` **is never set**. This is the shape this file records most often — *the call succeeds, nothing throws, the response is structurally valid, and the content is dead* — and the catch's comment makes it look like **the design**: it does skip when the formula is genuinely unavailable, but it also skips when the formula is available, and nothing anywhere can tell the two apart. Same family as cycle 171's `think-pipeline` `getFormulaBridge` and cycle 179's `value-internalizer` `_makeScoreResult`, where the guard itself was the broken thing. The fix is a function-local `require('../formula/formula-bridge.js')`, the same shape as `src/core/engine-memory.js:751`; both reasons for not putting it at module top are written into the source comment — a top-level require changes this module's load timing (heartflow lazy-loads it), and if `formula-bridge` itself failed to load, a top-level require would stop **the whole detector** from starting, when all that is wanted here is "skip the phase computation when the formula is unavailable". Measured: **0 of 12** history records carried `phaseTransition` before, **12 of 12** after, with real `ginzburgLandau` output. `test/sustained-drift-detector-contract.test.js` (5 cases) locks it: the phase result must really exist (not an empty object or placeholder), must match a direct `bridge.ginzburgLandau` call **word for word** (so a fake implementation cannot buy the lock), the import must appear before the call site, deleting the import must turn case ① red (proving the lock can actually see this defect), and a boundary case that the formula being genuinely unavailable must **not** throw outward — the catch's legitimate use must survive the fix. Mutation-verified in two directions: restoring the defect (deleting the import) turns **5/5** red; breaking the tolerance (requiring a non-existent path) turns **4/5** red by name. **Two errors in my own lock, both corrected and recorded.** (i) Case ② recomputed with the `0.95` I had fed in as if it were `emaScore`, while the code uses `this._ema()`'s smoothed value (`0.5`) — `reducedTemp` differed by exactly 1.5, and the failure looked *exactly* like "the implementation is fake". (ii) Case ③ used `indexOf` to find the call site and hit **the same name inside my own comment**, written above the import line, so `before` never contained the import and the case was unconditionally red; changed to `lastIndexOf`. **The B-class anchor cascade fired for the eighth/ninth time** (cycles 148/153/156/159/167/170/171/179): moving this module from B to C took B **100 → 99** and C **268 → 269**, SKILL.md's two coverage readings went stale, and the audit-family tests went **12 → 27** red; syncing the two readings returned them to the 12-item baseline. **One probe-pollution event found and repaired, and it is the shape 铁律 0 exists for:** mid-`run-all`, SKILL.md's `Engine version` row was left at the injected `9.9.9`. It was restored precisely to `1.0.73`, and a re-run of `run-all` left the row clean — a single event, not reproduced. Gate: verify **15/15**; run-all **1863 / 12 / 1875** with the 12 failures identical to baseline on both channels; audit **exit 0, 92 claims / 87 consistent / 0 mismatched**.
- **Cycle 189 fixed the numbers in the non-`.md` carriers; cycle 190 found the same carriers' *claims about capability and compliance* had no support at all — and the audit was blind to it for two independent reasons that compounded.** `doc-honest-numbers`, 190th cycle. (a) **`.well-known/agent-card.json`'s `complianceStandards` listed four standards** — GB/T 42497-2023 / GB/Z 185-2026 / ISO/IEC 27001 / NIST AI RMF — and a whole-repo grep (excluding `data/`, `src/data/`, and the claiming file itself) found traces for **only the first**, which has a real mapping document at `compliance/gb-agent-security-mapping.md`. The other three existed **only inside the claim**: no mapping document, no implementation, no test. An A2A discovery file was telling every external agent "I conform to these three standards" with nothing behind it. (b) **`compliance/gb-agent-security-mapping.md` was frozen whole at 2026-08-27** — the same moment as agent-card's `version: "6.7.5"` — carrying `45维判别` ×4 (measured **54**), `343 测试用例` (measured **1858**), `版本 v6.7.0`, seven tool names still on the `heartflow_` prefix when **all 181** tools are now `aspira_`, and a reference to `references/discriminator-dimensions.md` **which does not exist**. The audit read 88/88 green throughout, for **two reasons that compounded**: ① its Chinese dimensions pattern was `(\d[\d,]*)\s*个\s*(?:discrimination\s*)?维度` — it *requires* the character 个, while line 872 of the same file's comment says "对中文允许『维度』省略『个』" — **the comment knew what to do and the code did not do it** (the cycle-16 shape), and worse, in `45维判别` the character after 维 is 判, not 度, so the two characters 维度 never even appear consecutively; ② the mapping document *is* an engine self-description (its title is 新愿 Aspira × 国标安全映射表) but `SELF_DESC_DOCS` held only five top-level files, and line 691's `!f.includes(path.sep) &&` made **any subdirectory path added to that list permanently unreachable** — a dead entry point where "added to the whitelist" has no effect. The fix has four parts: the three unsupported standards are removed from agent-card (the honest minimum — writing three mapping documents would require product judgement this cycle does not have); the mapping document's stale readings are all set to measured values and its dangling reference now points at `AGENTS.md`'s real 54-dimension tables; the audit gains a single-character 维 pattern with a negative lookahead `维(?！度)` so it does not double-report the forms the old pattern already owns, **scoped by role rather than by path shape or a word list** — top-level self-description claims are about aspira by construction (cycle 9's conclusion), while a subdirectory document's `N维` defaults to a local count; and `SELF_DESC_DOCS` membership alone now decides, dropping the dead `!f.includes(path.sep)`. **Two instrument corrections, both this file's most-repeated lesson.** (i) The first widened pattern was `(\d[\d,]*)\s*维度` and it caught **nothing** — the probe said so in one line, because 维度 never appears consecutively in `54维判别`; the predicate has to land on the single character 维. (ii) Widening unconditionally produced **six new "mismatches", every one from `skills/` subdirectory manuals** — `56题 × 6维度`, `5维度核心能力专项测试`, `测试用例: 7维度 × 3用例`, `长度/语言/风格/格式/翻译方向 5维度` — those are *those skills' own test-set sizes*, and `SCOPED_BEFORE` holds only four English words (tier/across/within/between) with no Chinese counterpart for 题/用例/方向. That is **the sixth instance of a gate splitting by language**; the fix is not to enumerate Chinese measure words into `SCOPED_BEFORE` (an enumerating filter leaks once per new unit, as cycle 8 warned) but to split by scope. **And one existing lock had pinned the old behaviour by its literal shape, not its invariant.** `test/audit-gate-scope.test.js`'s `自述文档标志只对顶层文件生效` asserted the source contains `!f.includes(path.sep) && SELF_DESC_DOCS.has(f)` and went stably red; the invariant it was protecting — "a subdirectory README.md is not engine self-description" — **still holds** under the whitelist, so the case now locks the invariant behaviourally: the flag must be decided by whitelist membership, must not degrade to unconditional `true`, and `skills/` documents must contribute **zero** `dimensions` claims. Mutation-verified by injecting `SELF_DESCRIPTION_DOC = true`, which turns it red by name. `test/non-md-carrier-claims.test.js` (5 cases) locks the new contract: every `complianceStandards` entry must have a repository trace (excluding `test/` — **the first version forgot that exclusion and the fabricated standard `ISO/IEC 99999-2099`'s only "trace" was the test file itself**, the instrument searching for itself), every `features` entry must be registered in an `IMPL` table with a real implementation (an unregistered name now **fails** rather than being skipped — the first version skipped it, and mutation D, adding a `quantum-telepathy` feature, passed 5/5 in silence), the mapping document's 维 readings must equal the measurement, and every backticked file reference in it must exist. Gate: verify **15/15**; run-all **1853 → 1858 / 12 / 1865 → 1870** with the 12 failures identical to baseline on both channels; audit **exit 0, 88 → 92 claims / 87 consistent / 0 mismatched**. One concurrency artefact confirmed again: `identity-domains-consistency` went red once under `run-all` and 4/4 green when run alone.
- **The audit only scans `.md`, and two of the numbers a caller sees first were frozen years behind in files it cannot see.** `doc-honest-numbers`, 189th cycle. Cycles 7/8/9/10/11/12/19/176/187/188 had each asked the audit a different question (its keys, its patterns, its gates, its file set, measured-vs-patterned, the formula library's three numbers, its exemption list, measured-but-never-compared, cross-document contradictions, denominator self-consistency). This cycle asked cycle 10's inverse: cycle 10 established that the audit scans `.md` and nothing else — **so what does "only `.md`" leave out?** Two real defects, both in carriers a caller reads before it ever opens a markdown file. (a) **`package.json`'s `description`** — the paragraph shown on the npm page — claimed `51 discrimination dimensions, 14-layer output pipeline (12 input / 13 draft / 14 output), … 179 MCP tools` against a measured **54 / 17-layer set (11 per input call, 12 draft, 13 output) / 181**. Three numbers stale at once. (b) **`.well-known/agent-card.json`** — the standard A2A (agent-to-agent) discovery path, read by any agent that discovers Aspira through that protocol — claimed `version "6.7.5"`, `45维判别`, `discriminationDimensions 45`, `tools 153`, `tools.count 153`. Five numbers wrong, and the version was **frozen at CHANGELOG.md's `[6.7.5] - 2026-08-27`**, dozens of releases behind the package version. Both files sat behind an audit that read 88/88 green while npm's description and the A2A discovery card disagreed with each other and with the engine. **A file set is an enumerating filter, and this one enumerates by extension** — cycle 10 found the denominator inflated fourfold by 682 duplicate copies; this cycle found the same decision's other face, where a claim outside the extension is not under-counted, it is *unseen*, and an all-green report cannot tell the difference. The sharpest detail: agent-card's `version` was a **sixth copy of the version**, while convention #1 names five (VERSION / package.json / src/core/version.js / SKILL.md / runtime) — a copy the convention does not cover is the twin of cycle 13's *writer with no verifier*. The fix is three parts: both files' numbers set to measured values; `sync-version.js` gained a fourth target (agent-card's `version`) because **a verifier with no writer turns red on every bump**, which is a red that trains people to ignore the lock; and `test/non-md-doc-claims.test.js` (4 cases) pins every number against a **measurement**, never a literal, so "the document is wrong" and "the engine changed" stay distinguishable. **One instrument correction worth recording, because it is this file's most-repeated lesson:** the lock's first version measured MCP tools by regex-scraping `HANDLERS` keys out of `src/mcp-server.js` and got **179 against a true 181** — two keys' spelling does not match that regex. It would have "corrected" a *right* document to a wrong number. The measurement now uses the audit's own source (`src/mcp/tools-registry.js`'s `TOOLS.length`), and the divergence is disclosed in the code rather than quietly dropped. Mutation-verified in four directions (description tools, description dimensions, agent-card version, agent-card tools.count), each turning the matching case red by name. Gate: verify **15/15**; run-all **1849 → 1853 / 12 / 1861 → 1865** with the 12 failures identical to baseline item for item on both channels; audit **exit 0, 88 claims / 83 consistent / 0 mismatched**.
- **The audit's own denominator composition contained a branch that is structurally incapable
  of ever being non-zero — and it printed a `0` that read exactly like a measured exclusion.**
  `doc-honest-numbers`, 188th cycle. Cycles 7/8/9/10/11/19/176 had each asked the audit a
  different question (which keys are unmeasured, which numeric forms exist, which gates can
  never pass a claim, which files are scanned, measured-vs-patterned, what is exempted, which
  measurements are taken but never compared). This cycle asked the one never asked: **is the
  denominator the audit reports about itself actually self-consistent?** The answer exposed a
  dead branch. The composition line reports four mutually-exclusive classes — auditable,
  percentage, version-number/date, inside code fences — and the third has **never once been
  non-zero**. Exhaustively verified by construction: `NUMISH` requires *a number immediately
  followed by a unit word* (个/条/行/层/维/模块/工具/路由/`tests?`/…), while a version number's
  digit run is not adjacent to the unit word at all — in `1.0.70 modules` the regex matches
  `70 modules`, not `1.0.70`, so `/v?\d+\.\d+/` can never be tested; dates are the same shape.
  Hand-built `1.0.70 modules` / `v1.0.70 tools` / `2026-10-09 tests` / `>= 18.17 modules` and
  **not one** hit fragment contained a version-or-date form. So the printed
  "版本号/日期 0" does not mean "this class was actively excluded and measured at zero" — it
  means *the predicate cannot fire*. **A predicate that cannot fire and a predicate that
  fires-and-measures-zero are indistinguishable in an output that prints only the number**,
  which is the cycle-176 `measured-but-never-compared` shape in its dead-branch form: that
  time a number was measured and never compared; this time a number was printed that was
  never measurable at all. The predicate is deliberately kept (it is harmless and states
  intent), but the report now appends the reason whenever that class is zero, and a new
  self-consistency assertion requires `rawClaims === 四项和` — a composition that is not
  internally consistent is worse than no composition. `test/audit-denominator-self-consistency.test.js`
  (5 cases) locks it in four directions: source-level presence of both the conditional
  explanation and the invariant, behavioural appearance of the explanation in real output, a
  mutation that breaks the sum and must turn the audit red by name, and a reverse proof that
  stripping only the assertion block makes the same mutation silent again. **One residual
  recorded, not closed:** the audit's own `run-all` spawn intermittently measures one case
  fewer than a direct run (this cycle: 1843/13 twice against 1844/12 from both a direct run and
  an exact spawn-form replica; `ASPIRA_AUDIT_SKIP_TESTS=1` gave 88/88 with 0 mismatches, so the
  document claims are all correct and the divergence is entirely spawn-side). Cycle 29/142
  located and fixed a git-`PATH` dependency of the same visible symptom and the intermittent
  tail survived; its root cause is still not established.
- **Reverse-enumerating the audit's own key set found two measurements that were taken every
  run and locked nothing — one of them the number convention #1 names explicitly.** Eleventh
  consecutive `doc-honest-numbers` cycle, and deliberately a *hardening* cycle rather than a
  new angle: the previous four had asked the audit about its keys (cycle 7), its patterns
  (cycle 8), its gates (cycle 9) and its file set (cycle 10), and all four were now covered.
  So this cycle diffed `pats`' key set against the keys `measure()` actually assigns. Of 32
  assigned keys, 21 had no pattern — and most of those are legitimately intermediate
  (`depsDeclared`, `depsOptional`, `nodeScannedFiles`, `instantInstallWorks`), inputs to a
  real key rather than claims of their own. But two were **current claims in the documents
  with nothing checking them**: `version`, which convention #1 singles out in its own words
  (*"Never hardcode the version. `VERSION` is the single source of truth… `package.json` and
  `SKILL.md` must match"*) while `measure()` has been reading it and the audit has been
  printing it all along — **measured, displayed, and never compared**; and `testsFailed`,
  parsed by the very same regex that produces `m.tests` and then dropped on the floor, so the
  "0 failing" in README, SKILL.md and CURRENT_STATE.md was a sentence that never had to be
  true. Both confirmed by injection: `v1.0.0` → `v9.9.9` and `0 failing` → `7 failing` each
  left the audit at 78/78 with **zero** mismatches. This is the cycle-7 `domains` shape
  again — a number that is measured is not a number that is locked — except this time it hit
  the one number the repository's own first convention names. **The version fix is the
  interesting one, because the obvious pattern is the wrong one.** Matching a bare
  `v?\d+\.\d+\.\d+` looks like coverage and is a trap: the scanned documents contain
  **551** version-shaped strings, and nearly all are roadmap and architecture milestones
  (`ARCHITECTURE.md`'s v6.3.0 / v6.4.0 / v6.5.0, `ROADMAP.md`'s v6.0.6) that are not the
  package version. That is the 26th instrument failure's lesson restated — an enumerating
  filter leaks once per new way of narrating, and version numbers have a dozen narrations.
  So the pattern only accepts two **labelled** forms: `| Engine version | 1.0.0 |` in
  SKILL.md's metric table and `> 版本 | v1.0.0` at the head of CURRENT_STATE.md. **And one
  near-miss is disclosed rather than quietly included:** `REFLECTION.md` carries
  `| 版本 | 6.2.7 |` under `## 现在（2026-07-25）`, a point-in-time snapshot pinned inside a
  "past / present / future" reflection document — checking it against today would
  manufacture a false alarm, and the bare Chinese `版本` label is no guarantee of "engine
  version" in an arbitrary table. Both new keys were verified by live injection in both
  directions, and the failing-count check could only be verified *without*
  `ASPIRA_AUDIT_SKIP_TESTS` — that flag nulls `m.testsFailed` along with `m.tests`, so under
  it a wrong failure count lands in "unmeasurable" instead of "mismatched", the same probe
  artefact recorded in cycle 9. Suite 1233 → **1239** (`test/audit-unlocked-measurements.test.js`,
  6 cases); verify 14/14; audit **82/82**; corpus was unchanged (106 benign / FP 0.9%,
  41 malicious / recall 100%); no `src/` change, so MCP was not restarted. **One process
  note, recorded because it is the same class as everything above:** while live-injecting
  three documents I used a shell loop whose `IFS='|'` split on the pipe characters *inside*
  the markdown table rows, which silently corrupted `CURRENT_STATE.md`'s version line into
  ` v1.0.0| v1.0.0`. It was caught by re-reading `git diff` before running the gate and
  repaired, but the lesson is the one this file keeps relearning — **a tool that reports
  success while having written the wrong thing is worse than one that fails**, and here the
  shell's own success message was the misleading part.



- **A persistence layer that was one `path.join` away from writing outside its own directory, and that silently rewrote any caller object carrying a `v` or `q` key.** Thirty-first cycle, and the engine chose `test-coverage-gap` (composite 0.73, confidence 0.70) — the **third production confirmation** of cycle 26's recent-window rotation (30 adversarial-robustness, 31 test-coverage-gap). The cycle opened by measuring the slice's own claim, and the first measurement was already a correction to the slice: **kv-cache was not an uncovered module.** The cycle-28 census counted it among the 164 `src/` modules with no test reference, but `test/kv-cache-persistor.test.js` has existed with ten cases all along — it builds its require as `path.join(ROOT, "src", "memory", "kv-cache.js")`, segmented, so a grep on the module *name* cannot see it. That is the same shape as the `spawnsDocAudit()` idiom gap this file already recorded, and it means the "164 uncovered" figure is inflated by an unknown amount. Probing the module by *calling* it then found **three real defects**, and the first is the serious one. (a) **All four public methods joined their arguments straight into a path with no sanitisation.** Measured: `save("../escape", "k", v)` lands at the parent of cacheDir and `save("..", "k", v)` at the parent itself — both **outside cacheDir**; `save("a/b", ...)` lands inside but at an uncontrolled depth. (`save("/tmp/x", ...)` is the opposite case — `path.join` normalises the leading slash away, so the absolute escape is the one that gets contained; the danger is specifically the `..` prefix.) Since `sessionId` and `key` can arrive from MCP tool arguments or any caller, this is an arbitrary-directory-write primitive in a persistence layer, not a theoretical one. (b) **The serialisation had no envelope, so `load()` read caller data as its own framing.** `save` wrote `{q, shape}` or `{v}` for arrays but bare `JSON.stringify(tensor)` for everything else, while `load` interpreted `data.q` (dequantise) and `data.v` (unwrap). So a caller object carrying a `v` or `q` key was **silently rewritten**: measured, `save(s,k,{v:1})` -> `load` returns the number `1` — the object is gone — and `save(s,k,{q:[1,2]})` returns a dequantised array. `if (data.q)` also truthiness-tested, so `{q:0}` fell through to a third branch: three results for one input family. That is *the call succeeds, nothing throws, the response is structurally valid, and the content is wrong*, with silent data corruption as the payload. The fix is an explicit tagged envelope `{_KV:1, kind, payload}` dispatched on `kind` — `kind='array'` unwraps the internal framing, `kind='value'` returns the payload untouched. A wrapper alone was **not** enough and the middle attempt proved it: array payloads *are* `{q,shape}`/`{v}`, so re-applying the old logic to the payload re-broke every array round-trip and would have re-broken `{q:[1,2]}` caller objects. Legitimately-read legacy files still work through a narrow fallback that requires `Array.isArray` — which is exactly what keeps it from colliding with user data `{v:1}` / `{q:0}` (measured both directions; zero `.kv` files exist in the repo and the default dir is in `os.tmpdir()`, so nothing real was ever corrupted). (c) **`getStats().hitRate` was a string while every other field in the same object was a number.** Measured: `typeof === "number"` false, `hitRate.toFixed(2)` throws `TypeError`, and `hitRate > 0.5` returns false at exactly 0.50 (only accidentally right, via coercion). `decision-engine.js`'s own `sdtAnalyze` docstring says `@param {number} hitRate`. The lock is the existing `test/kv-cache-persistor.test.js`, grown from **10** cases to **14**: one case per defect plus a reverse control asserting legal `sessionId`/`key` shapes still round-trip, and one pinning that every `getStats()` field except `version` is a number. **Three process notes, each a re-payment.** (a) *The gate went to 1391/8 and the cause was the unsynced test count, not what I first thought.* I had branched on the doc-probe lock — a 60-second lock-acquisition timeout — and blamed a lock orphaned by my own SIGTERM'd run. Measuring decided it: the lock file was already released and no probe process survived, so that theory was simply wrong, and reverting to `git HEAD` gave a clean 1395/0 baseline. The real chain was that four new cases moved the measured count 1395 -> 1399 while the documents still said 1395, the audit therefore reported eleven mismatches, and the doc-probe tests that require an all-green audit failed as a cascade. Syncing the four current-claim sites fixed it. **A red gate whose message names a subsystem is not evidence about that subsystem.** (b) *My own new test over-asserted and I let the measurement correct it.* I had `con` in the must-be-rejected key list; on POSIX `con.kv` is a perfectly legal filename and it stored and read back fine, with no harm I could measure. It moved to the reverse control instead — the same family as asserting a number nobody measured. (c) *Adding a private helper moved a public documented number.* `heartflow.routes()` builds its table with `Object.getOwnPropertyNames(proto).filter(m => m !== "constructor" && ...)` — **no filtering of `_`-prefixed names** — so adding one `_safeSegment` method to a class incremented the measured "dispatch routes" from 1510 to 1511, deterministically, and reddened the audit. The instrument is wrong (a public claim perturbed by implementation detail), but fixing it means re-baselining the count across all 132 modules, which is a `doc-honest-numbers` job. This cycle only avoided adding to the public surface: the sanitiser became a **module-level** function, matching the shape of `quantizeArray`/`dequantizeArray` in the same file, and routes returned to 1510. Gate measured against this cycle's starting point: verify **15/15**, run-all **1395 -> 1399** with 0 failures, audit **85/85 with exit code 0** after the sync, corpus **identical to baseline item for item** (106 benign / FP 0.9%, 41 malicious / 41 intercepted / 100.0%; evasion 85% / 71% / 95% unchanged). `src/` changed, but no MCP tool definition, handler mapping or permission contract was added or modified and `routes()` measures the same 1510, so MCP was **not** restarted (recorded as not-performed, not as performed). **Three gaps recorded rather than closed:** `routes()` counting private helpers as dispatch routes; the cycle-28 "164 uncovered modules" census being inflated by `path.join`-segmented requires; and `_doc-probe-lock.js`'s `STALE_MS = 120000` exceeding the 60000ms patience of its own callers, which means an orphaned lock can never be reclaimed inside a caller's wait window.
- **Three of the 181 advertised MCP tools were structurally incapable of working — one because its handler read a field off a `sessionId` string, two because they read fields that only `start()` ever assigns.** Twenty-ninth cycle, and the engine chose `mcp-tool-enhancement` (composite 0.74, confidence 0.65) — the **fourth consecutive cycle on a different slice** (26 `doc-honest-numbers`, 27 `dimension-health-audit`, 28 `test-coverage-gap`, 29 `mcp-tool-enhancement`), the third production confirmation of cycle 26's recent-window rotation. The cycle opened by measuring the slice's own claim: **181** tool definitions and **181** handler keys, one-for-one, which looks complete. Counting is not calling. Invoking each of the four tools the repository had already *recorded* as suspect returned **structurally valid errors** from three of them: `aspira_boundary_check` -> `{error:'boundaryGuard not loaded'}`, `aspira_decision_decide` -> `{error:'decision.decide not available'}`, `aspira_decision_feedback` -> `{error:'decisionFeedback not initialized'}`. No throw, no timeout, no protocol failure — the call succeeds and the content is dead, which is the contract-mismatch family this file keeps recording, and the three turned out to be **one root cause in two shapes**. (a) **`aspira_boundary_check` could never have worked, on any input, ever.** Its handler is declared `(args, hf)` and reads `hf.boundaryGuard`; but the central dispatch calls `handler(args, sessionId)`, and `sessionId` is `crypto.randomUUID()` — a **string** (or `url.searchParams.get('sessionId')`, also a string). Reading `.boundaryGuard` off a string is `undefined`, so `bg` is `null` and the tool returns its error unconditionally. This is not "occasionally fails to load"; it is *a string where an object was wanted*, and no amount of waiting or restarting changes it. (b) **`decision` / `boundaryGuard` / `decisionFeedback` are assigned only inside `heartflow`'s `start()`** — which opens with `if (this.started) return;` — and all three handlers construct a fresh `new hf.Aspira({rootPath, silent:true})` and then read the field **without ever calling `start()`**. Measured both ways: without `start()` the three fields are `null` / `undefined` / `undefined`; after `start()` they are `OK` / `OK` / `OK` with `_initErrors` at 0. The `decisionFeedback` assignment's `catch` is **empty** (`/* decisionFeedback optional */`), so even a genuine load failure would leave no trace anywhere — the field would simply stay `null` and the tool would keep reporting "not initialized" forever. Both are fixed: `aspira_boundary_check` now builds its own instance like its two siblings, and all three call `start()` before reading (wrapped in a `try`, because `start()` itself throws `Cannot read properties of undefined (reading 'slice')` near its tail — *after* the modules are wired, so swallowing it is correct and measured). Measured after: `boundary_check` returns a real verdict (`ALLOW`, `outside agent territories`), `decision_decide` returns a real decision — and notably **refuses to pick** when the options are indistinguishable (`甲=0.8, 乙=0.8` -> `chosen: null`, "拒绝任意挑选"), which is the discriminator behaving correctly rather than a failure — and `decision_feedback` returns a real weight adjustment (`1 -> 0.9`). `aspira_benchmark_run` still answers `未找到数据包`, but that is an **honest error**: the directory is genuinely empty, so it was left alone. The new lock is `test/mcp-advertised-tools-live.test.js` (**5** cases) and it pins the root cause rather than the symptom: case ① asserts the three tools return non-error content for a legal call; case ② asserts `decision_decide` genuinely discriminates (the obviously-better option must be chosen); case ③ is source-level and asserts each handler calls `start()` **and** that no handler still declares a second parameter as an engine; case ④ is a *reverse control* asserting the dispatch still passes `sessionId` from `crypto.randomUUID()` — because case ③'s conclusion is derived from that fact, and a "must" whose premise has silently changed is a check that can never fail; case ⑤ pins TOOLS/HANDLERS one-for-one. Mutation-verified: removing `start()` and restoring the string-as-engine signature drops the lock to 4/5 **by name**, and restoring returns 5/5. Gate measured against this cycle's starting point: verify **15/15**, run-all **1390 -> 1395** with 0 failures, audit **85/85 with exit code 0, 6/6 green** after syncing 1390 -> 1395 across ten sites, corpus **identical to baseline item for item** (106 benign / FP 0.9%, 41 malicious / recall 100.0%). `src/` changed, but only inside three handler bodies — no tool definition, handler mapping or permission contract was added or modified, TOOLS/HANDLERS remain 181/181 one-for-one, so MCP was **not** restarted (recorded as not-performed, not as performed). **Two process notes, and the first one nearly destroyed the file this cycle was about.** (a) *My mutation rollback truncated `src/mcp-server.js` to zero bytes.* The restore was written as `io.open(p,'w').write(io.open(backup).read())` — Python evaluates the left side **first**, truncating the target to 0 bytes, and only then evaluates the right-hand argument; the backup had never been created because the mutation's anchor assertion had failed, so the `FileNotFoundError` was thrown with the file already empty. Recovery: `git show HEAD:src/mcp-server.js` (186,236 bytes) — safe here because cycles 26, 27 and 28 demonstrably never touched this file (26 changed no `src/`, 27 changed only `index.js`, 28 changed only `memory-encrypt.js`), so HEAD *was* the pre-cycle state — then replayed both fixes verbatim from the edit record and re-measured all three tools live and the lock at 5/5. The lesson is the cycle-27 sentence sharpened: **the pre-change text must be held in memory, not reconstructed from a file round-trip**, and version control is a safety net only when you can *prove* it holds the last good state. The redo kept `orig` in a JavaScript variable across mutate -> test -> restore, so no filesystem round-trip could intervene. (b) *The audit went red about one run in six for a reason that is not probe pollution.* Six consecutive audits gave 5 green and 1 red reading `CURRENT_STATE.md 声称 tests passed = 1395, 实测 1394` — and reproducing the audit's **own spawn form exactly** (`spawnSync('node',['test/run-all.js'],{cwd,encoding,timeout,stdio:['ignore','pipe','ignore']})`) returns 1395/0 every time. So the 1394 is a genuine intermittent one-case shortfall in the measured count, not document drift and not probe debris; the likely source is a test whose case count is derived from repository state (there are hundreds of untracked `data/_tc_*` directories). It is recorded rather than papered over: the documents claim 1395 because that is what the suite measures, and a discrepancy that appears one run in six is a *real* defect in the honest-numbers contract — any assertion written against the documented number will red intermittently.
  **第一百四十二轮根因已定位并修复: 那个 ~1/6 间歇红不是并发、不是文档漂移、
  也不是"用例数由仓库状态推导"—— 是 git 可执行文件依赖了调用方的 PATH。**
  `test/security-audit.test.js` 的 S2 用例用 `execFileSync('git', …)` 查
  commit/tag 里是否有当前版本，而 git 在 `/usr/bin/git`、测试进程的 PATH 只含
  `/usr/local/bin`(由调用方环境决定)。于是**这个检查的可用性取决于谁调用它**:
  从 PATH 更全的入口跑就绿，从 run-all 跑就 `spawnSync git ENOENT`。
  它同时踩中本仓库两条最贵的教训:
  ① 失败只出现在通道 b 的文件级汇总行("安全审计回归: 15 通过, 1 失败")，
  而"失败的测试:"清单**从不收录**它 —— 所以前六轮每一次"清单与基线逐条一致"
  的比对都是真的，而总数一直在抖。铁律 3 的第二个通道正是为这个形状而设，
  本轮就是靠它定位的。
  ② 文档的测试数读数是**以清单为准**同步的，于是它带着这个入口依赖性一起漂
  (1638/12 与 1637/13 之间)。一个数字 measured 但 never compared，
  比一个错数字更难发现 —— 它每次重跑都可能变成对的。
  修法: S2 显式在若干标准位置找 git 二进制并用绝对路径; 修后连续三次 run-all
  与三次审计稳定在 1641/12 与 87/87。新锁
  `test/git-binary-path-independence.test.js`(3 例)钉住源级不回归、在**刻意
  收窄 PATH** 的子进程里必须全绿、以及"退回裸 git 必须红"的自证。
  **教训的一般形式: 一个测试若依赖外部可执行文件，它继承的是调用方的环境
  而不是仓库的状态 —— 这类间歇性永远无法用"重跑一次看看"定位，
  只能把环境收窄后复现。** **One gap recorded rather than closed:** the audit's ~1-in-6 `tests=1394` reading is unlocated(根因已定位, 见上一段); `aspira_benchmark_run` needs a data package before it can do anything; the dimension-health instrument's `evidence` false alarm (7630 signals / 0 findings) is still unfixed; and the 164 uncovered `src/` modules still have exactly **one** of them covered.
- **The slice that rotated in found a memory module that would have handed a caller the ciphertext envelope instead of its contents — and the same module's two read paths disagreed about the same input.** Twenty-eighth cycle, and the engine chose `test-coverage-gap` (composite 0.77, confidence 0.70) — the **third consecutive cycle on a different slice** (26 `doc-honest-numbers`, 27 `dimension-health-audit`, 28 `test-coverage-gap`), which is the second production confirmation of cycle 26's recent-window rotation. The cycle opened by measuring the slice's own claim: of **383** `src/` modules, **164** have no test reference at all, and all 164 load cleanly — so the gap is real and is not "modules that cannot be required". Grouped by family it is `memory` 22, `cortex` 20, `core` 19, `shield` 12, `emotion` 10, `identity` 9, `formula`/`reasoning` 8 each, `dream`/`workflow` 7 each. Requiring 22 modules to "cover" the `memory` family would have been coverage theatre — it proves they load and nothing else — so the cycle picked the one module in that family whose contract is both safety-critical and crisply round-trippable: `src/memory/memory-encrypt.js`, which exports `encryptJSON`/`decryptJSON` plus async twins. Probing it found **two real defects**. (a) **An unknown `_enc` version is silently treated as plaintext.** `decryptJSON` dispatches on exactly two values — `HEARTFLOW_v1` -> decrypt, `PLAINTEXT_FALLBACK` -> unwrap — and everything else falls through to `return parsed`. Measured: change `_enc` from `HEARTFLOW_v1` to `HEARTFLOW_v2` and the call returns `{"_enc":"HEARTFLOW_v2","iv":"...","authTag":"...","data":"<ciphertext>"}` — no throw, no warning, and the caller accepts the **ciphertext envelope as the memory content**. The trigger is not exotic: after a key rotation or algorithm bump raises `_enc` to v2, an older reader process hitting a new file gets no signal at all, and because the envelope is structurally valid JSON every downstream type check and assertion passes straight over it. That is the contract-mismatch family this file keeps recording — *the call succeeds, nothing throws, the response is structurally valid, and the content is dead* — now with a security payload. (b) **`decryptJSONAsync` is missing the `PLAINTEXT_FALLBACK` unwrap that `decryptJSON` has.** Source comparison found it in one line: the sync path has the `v6.0.50 M1` branch, the async path does not. The same degraded-plaintext input therefore yields the inner `data` from one path and the whole envelope from the other. Both are fixed: each path now fails loudly on any `HEARTFLOW_`-prefixed version it does not know (narrowly scoped to that namespace, so naturally-plaintext objects carrying an unrelated `_enc` field are not collateral damage), and the async path gained the missing unwrap. Measured after: round-trip deep-equal, tampered ciphertext throws, tampered authTag throws, forged envelope throws, natural plaintext passes through, non-JSON returns `null`, and sync/async agree on every input including the degraded-plaintext one. The new lock is `test/memory-encrypt-contract.test.js` (**5** cases), two-sided — the unknown-version case pins that both paths throw, the known-forms case pins that plaintext passthrough and round-trip were **not** broken by the guard, and a source-level case counts the guards and the unwraps so a future deletion fails by name. Mutation-verified by replacing the guard with `if (false)`: cases ① and ⑤ go red together, restoring returns 5/5. Gate measured against this cycle's starting point: verify **15/15**, run-all **1385 -> 1395** with 0 failures, audit **85/85 with exit code 0, five for five** after syncing 1385 -> 1395 across eight sites in README/SKILL/CURRENT_STATE/AGENTS, corpus **identical to baseline item for item** (106 benign / FP 0.9%, 41 malicious / recall 100.0%). `src/` changed, but only in `memory-encrypt`'s read path — no MCP tool definition or handler was added or modified, so the tool inventory and permission contract are unchanged and MCP was **not** restarted (recorded as not-performed, not as performed). **Two process notes.** (a) *My own probe deleted a file that was someone else's contract.* To measure loadability I required all 164 uncovered modules in-process; one of them writes `aspira-audit-report.md` as a side effect. I deleted that file as probe garbage — and `test/audit-doc-coverage.test.js` went red immediately with `豁免的 aspira-audit-report.md 应真实存在`, because it is a **legitimate generated artefact named in the audit's exemption table** and the exemption had gone dangling. Regenerated with `node src/repo-audit.js` and re-measured 8/8. *Before deleting a file, ask whether it is part of someone else's contract.* (b) The mutation proof rolled back by rewriting the guard to `if (false)` rather than by `git checkout` — the cycle-27 lesson held: version control restores the last commit, not last-good state. **One gap recorded rather than closed:** 164 uncovered modules and this cycle covered **one** of them. All five new cases pin that single module's contract; the remaining 163 — including the 21 other `memory` modules and the 19 in `core` — are untouched, and the honest count of new coverage is 1 module, not 164.
- **The rotation fix held on its first real run — and the slice it rotated to immediately found a dimension that could move the gate while being structurally unable to say anything.** Twenty-seventh cycle, and the engine chose `dimension-health-audit` (composite 0.75, confidence 0.60) — **the first time in nine cycles it did not choose `doc-honest-numbers`**, and the first confirmation that cycle 26's recent-window rotation works outside a synthetic history. This cycle opened on the instrument rather than on a hunch: `scripts/dimension-health-audit.js` reported `perfect_error` with **143 signals and 0 findings**, flagged "触发但从不推 finding". The registration audit explains how that is possible — `perfect_error` has a `dimMap` key, is one of the 54 runtime keys of `dimensions{}`, is one of the 26 in `VERIFY_DIMS`, has its own sentence in `GUIDANCE_MAP`, has its own gate rule in `index.js`, and `pipeline.js:197` pushes the gate to `rewrite` off `dimensions.perfect_error.count >= 2`. The one thing it does not have is a push block; `grep "dimension: 'perfect_error'"` was empty. So it is **the only dimension that can change `gate.action` while being structurally unable to produce a finding.** The consequence is not a missing line in a report: the caller receives `action: 'rewrite'` and a `findings` array that does not name the reason, so `findings.some(f => VERIFY_DIMS.has(f.dimension))` cannot see it, and the `GUIDANCE_MAP` sentence *"补充可验证的来源和数据，对无法验证的断言降低确定性…"* is **dead code that has never once executed** — a repair instruction written, maintained, and translated, that no finding could ever collect. The fix is a dedicated push block in the existing pattern (`unsupported_claim` / `pseudo_causal` / `soft_deflection` all have one), gated on **the dimension's own grading** `pe.level !== 'pass'` (`perfect-error.js:194-198`: `count>=3` or 4+ with false precision → `high`; pseudo-authority plus false precision → `rewrite`; `count>=2` → `verify`) rather than `allDims`' `score >= 0.15`, which is calibrated for single-signal dimensions.
