- **A public method that promised "original in, strengthened out" wrote back into the original — and the same file proved the intent by deep-cloning everywhere else.** `test-coverage-gap`, 201st cycle, and the engine chose the same slice as cycles 194–200 (0.77, confidence 0 — a tie broken by `consequence_value`). The first eight cycles on this slice each opened with a different question for picking a module inside class B — *crisp contract* (191), *promised by full path in a document* (194), *exports pure functions and constant tables* (195), *highest `src/` fan-in* (196), *called directly by an MCP tool* (197), *error handling most likely to disguise failure* (198), *never validates its entry parameters* (199), *public methods all return the same constant* (200) — and all eight were used up. This cycle asked a ninth, and it is cycle 196's own question turned around: **which B-class module is reachable from the most *tested* modules through the require graph, rather than directly?** Cycle 196 ranked by *direct* requirers, so a module with one or two direct consumers that dozens of tested modules reach transitively was invisible to it. The instrument reuses the repository's own `coverage-sweep.js` classification rather than reimplementing `hasRequireOf` — my first copy of that predicate dropped the third (variable-binding) form and mis-classified **11 modules** as untested, which is the cycle-191 lesson in a new costume: *re-implementing an instrument to save a `require` is how you get a different answer than the instrument*. Measured on the sweep's own B/C/A (92/279/12), the winner was `src/reasoning/self-play.js` — **891 lines, the largest B module, zero test references, exactly one direct consumer** (`heartflow.js`, itself tested), reachable from **23 tested modules**, and genuinely live: `think-pipeline.js:72` and `:150` both call `engine.selfPlay.refine(...)`. It is not cycle 199's constructed-but-never-called shape. Probing it by **calling** it found the defect in one line. `defend()`'s first act was `const defended = { ...judgment }` — **a one-level shallow copy.** So when `_applyDefense()` wrote `judgment.chosenPath.metadata.defenses`, `defended.chosenPath` and the caller's `chosenPath` were *the same reference*, and that write landed on the caller's object. Measured: `challengeCount = 4 | addressed = 3`, and the caller's judgment silently gained `chosenPath.metadata.defenses` — while the method's own JSDoc calls the parameter 「原始判断」 and the return value `strengthened`. The contract was "input untouched, output strengthened". **The design intent is provable from inside the same file**: `refine()` opens with `let current = this._deepClone(judgment)`, carefully protecting its caller's object. One public method in the file guards the boundary and the other corrupts it. The fix is one line — `const defended = this._deepClone(judgment)` — and a shallower repair is provably insufficient: `chosenPath.metadata` and the `defenses` array itself are also shared references, so copying one level still accumulates onto the caller's array across repeated calls (measured). The module's own `_deepClone` is used rather than `structuredClone`, because `refine()` already applies the same transformation to the same object, so no new risk class is introduced, and because `_deepClone` returns non-objects untouched, which keeps `defend(42, …)`'s existing throw point where it was. **The lock's source-level case went red on its first run, for the sixth recorded time, because my own repair comment quoted the removed line verbatim** — the cycle-19/25/194/195/197 pattern, and the fix is the same each time: blank line comments before matching. **The second lock failure was a disclosure I had wrong, and only running it caught it:** I wrote that `refine(null)` throws. It does not — a single `null` argument is caught by the method's own empty-judgment guard. What throws is the *second* argument: `refine(judgment, null)` dies on `options.maxCycles`, because a default value covers *not passing the argument* and nothing else. That is the cycle-199/200 family, restated on a new module, and the test now pins both shapes — including an assertion that the single-argument form must **not** throw, so the disclosure cannot be satisfied by a wrong claim in either direction. `test/self-play-defend-purity.test.js` (11 cases) covers: the caller's judgment byte-identical after `defend()`; the nested `metadata.defenses` array not accumulating (the layer a shallow repair misses); `refine()` leaving its input alone; a reverse control that the strengthening still happens (`defenses.length > 0`, `confidence` still rises, `refined.chosenPath !== input.chosenPath` — reference independence, not just value equality); `defend()`'s own return contract unchanged; a source-level deep-clone requirement; a reverse proof that rebuilds the broken module **in `/tmp`** by rewriting its one relative `require`, so no mutated copy ever lands in `src/`; and four disclosures pinned as still-true. Mutation-verified on the real file: restoring `{ ...judgment }` turns **4** cases red by name — including the reverse proof's own anchor, which correctly reports "anchor missing" rather than passing silently — and the file was restored byte-identical. Gate: verify **15/15**; run-all **1956 → 1967 / 0 / 1967 with exit 0**, both channels empty (the 12-item baseline in `test-baseline.txt` is now fully stale — the ten `benchmark`-family entries were repaired in cycle 193 and the three `audit-drift-detection` ones passed this run). The B-class anchor cascade fired for the **eighteenth** time (148/153/156/159/167/170/171/179/191/193/194/195/196/197/198/199/200/201): moving `self-play.js` from B to C took B **92 → 91** and C **279 → 280**, and SKILL.md's coverage reading was synced. **The existing lock that pinned the cascade went red, and it went red for the reason it was built to go red** — `coverage-sweep-require-forms.test.js`'s denominator-honesty case asserts "the current B-class king must still be in B", and `self-play.js` *was* the king. Per the precedent set at cycles 148/156/159/167/170/171/179/182 the anchor moved to the new king (`src/identity/agent-philosophy.js`, 889 lines), with the cycle-201 note written into the test saying plainly that this cycle's lock covers **one** contract — `defend()`'s purity — and that the rest of the 891 lines is still untested. Suite **1956 → 1967**, all eleven new cases from the new lock. `src/` changed in one function body — no MCP tool definition, handler mapping or permission contract was added or modified, so MCP was **not** restarted (recorded as not-performed). **Two repairs carried over from earlier cycles and finished here, because this cycle was the first to touch the file they broke:** AGENTS.md's honest-limitations list had cycle 194's entry opening with a doubled `- **- **` marker and cycle 193's entry sitting at the very end of the list with **no list marker at all**, which markdown renders as a lazy continuation of cycle 200's entry. Both were introduced by my own earlier appends; both are fixed, and cycle 193's entry is back in chronological position between 192 and 194. **Four things measured this cycle and deliberately not fixed, each recorded rather than papered over.** ① **`refine()`'s `improvement` is structurally always 0, which makes both production consumers' `> 0` guard dead code.** `improvement = finalScore - initialScore`, both from `evaluateRobustness()`; and `_applyDefense()` writes only `chosenPath.metadata.defenses`, `judgment.metadata` and `confidence += 0.02` — **not one of the six `_scoreDimension` axes reads any of the three.** So a successful defense is invisible to the scorer and the score is invariant under refinement (measured across four judgment shapes: improvement `0, 0, 0, 0`). The consequence is not a cosmetic report: `think-pipeline.js:81` and `:159` are both `if (spResult && spResult.improvement > 0) { … }`, so "verification score too low → SelfPlay targeted refinement → re-verify" has **never once fired**. Making the scorer recognise a defense means deciding what a successful defense is *worth*, which is product judgement and 铁律 2's explicit exclusion; the test pins the current reading with an instruction to invert it if that ever changes. ② `defend()` returns `score: NaN` when there are no challenges (`0/0`), which lands in `refine()`'s `history[].defenseScore`. One line, but a different change from the one this cycle made, so it was left alone rather than bundled. ③ `_buildAlternative()` picks `promising` with `Math.random() > 0.4` — twelve identical calls produced **nine** distinct `promising` combinations, two fresh instances disagree on the first call, and the value directly selects the RL reward (0.7 vs 0.3). Same family as cycle 197's `spontaneous-restraint`, but that was another module in another cycle. ④ `refine(judgment, null)` and `defend(badShape, withChallenges)` still throw — the default-value-only defence family again. Neither is reachable from the two production call sites, which construct their own objects. **And a fifth thing, which is not a defect in the self-play module at all but was root-caused by this cycle and is the most valuable hand-off it leaves:** the suite’s own case count swings by exactly **13**, and AGENTS.md has recorded that swing as "root cause not established" for dozens of cycles. `emitResult()` in `test/run-all.js` takes its count from the **first** line of a child’s stdout matching `(\d+) 通过, (\d+) 失败`. But `test/git-binary-path-independence.test.js` spawns`security-audit.test.js` in a narrowed-PATH subprocess and, when that child fails, embeds the child’s own summary line inside its assertion message — which lands **before** the parent file’s own summary. The runner then books the child’s cases to the parent: measured this cycle,the suite reads one total with the child green and a total thirteen higher with it red, reproduced inboth directions and traced line-by-line in the output. **A counter that reads the first matching lineinstead of the one that belongs to it is a counter that reports another process’s work.** The repairis to match the harness’s own summary line rather than the first occurrence; it was deliberatelynot made this cycle because the one-minimal-change rule was spent on the defend purity fix, but it isthe reason the documented test count could not be trusted mid-cycle and it is the first thing thenext cycle should take apart. Two further readings were taken and are recorded rather than averaged away: an audit-spawn reading ten below the direct reading, after which two consecutive audit-form spawns and one direct run all agreed — so the lower figure is what the documents now claim, and the ten-case gap is disclosed as unexplained rather than smoothed over.
- **The audit read 97 for 97 green, and the instrument that produces one of those 97 could not see a ninth of the suite — because its regex read one language and took the first match.** `doc-honest-numbers`, 202nd cycle, and the engine chose it decisively for the first time in a while (0.77 / confidence 0.75, no tie to break). Cycles 7/8/9/10/11/12/19/176/187/188/189/190 had each asked the audit a different question about its keys, patterns, gates, file set, exemptions, unlocked measurements, cross-document contradictions, denominator composition and non-`.md` carriers. This cycle asked the one that had never been asked of a **green** report: *the numbers are all correct — but is the instrument that produces each number actually able to see the thing it claims to measure?* The audit reports `文档声称总数: 97 | 与实测一致: 97 | 不一致: 0`, exit 0, so every documented number matches its measurement. The defect was one layer below, in the measurement itself. The audit's `tests` and `testsFailed` keys are obtained by spawning `test/run-all.js` and reading its totals, and `run-all.js` obtained those totals from **one inline regular expression** inside `emitResult()`: `out.match(/(\d+) 通过, (\d+) 失败/)`. Probing it by *running all 394 test files individually* and comparing what that regex extracts against what each file actually prints found **two defects, both measured, neither suspected**. ① **It reads Chinese only.** Nineteen files print their results in English and therefore contributed **zero** cases to the suite total while exiting 0 with zero failures: `audit-regression: 86 passed, 0 failed`, `compliance: ═══ 27 passed / 0 failed ═══`, `recovered-modules-2: 23 passed, 0 failed`, `recovered-modules: 16 passed, 0 failed`, `35dim-integration: 13 passed, 0 failed`, `domain-blocklist-smoke: 10`, `gdi-to-router: 8`, `mcp-smoke: 8`, `metacognitive-executive: 8`, `core/output-checklist: 7`, `confidence-annotator: 4`, `decision-router: 4`, `module-health-self-heal: 4`, `self-state-persist: 4`, `stability-guard: 4`, `upgrade-engine-self-record: 4`, `smart-upgrade-cleanup: 3`, `judgment-engine: 2`, and `blindspot-upgrade: 7/7 passed`. **242 real, passing, executed test cases had never entered the documented count** — and the audit read green throughout, because the document and the instrument agreed with each other about a number that was wrong in the same direction. ② **It takes the first match, not the last.** This is the root cause cycle 201 located and handed off: `git-binary-path-independence.test.js` runs `security-audit.test.js` in a narrowed-PATH subprocess and, when that child fails, embeds the child's own summary line inside its assertion message — **before** the parent file's own summary. The runner then booked the child's 15 passes to a file that had run 2 cases, inflating the suite total by exactly 13 (1967 → 1980, reproduced in both directions and traced line by line). And `security-audit` fails deterministically whenever the version has been bumped but the tag not yet created — which is to say **every single upgrade cycle triggers this偏差, at exactly the moment the documented test count is being synced.** The fix is one new helper, `test/_child-summary.js`, which `emitResult()` now calls: it takes the **last** match, in four layers ordered by information strength — the harness's own `测试结果:` line (355 of 372 files print it), then any Chinese `N 通过, M 失败`, then English `N passed, M failed` or `N passed / M failed`, then `N/N passed`. Layered rather than "last match of anything", because the canonical Chinese line is the strongest signal and must not be displaced by an English sub-suite line printed after it. Measured after, with an exact per-file accounting rather than an estimate: 19 files recovered 242 cases, the new lock contributes 12, and the suite reads **1967 → 2221 passed / 0 failed / 2221 total, exit 0** — 242 + 12 = 254, closing to the digit. The four documentation sites were synced and the audit re-measured **97/97 with exit 0**. `test/child-summary-parse.test.js` (12 cases) locks both directions: all five measured summary shapes must be read; a decoy printed before the real summary must not win; multiple canonical lines must resolve to the last; the canonical Chinese form must outrank an English line printed after it; no summary at all must return `null` so the existing "child crashed" branch still fires; a reverse proof that inlines the **old predicate verbatim** and requires it to fail on both the English and the decoy shape (without this, "fixed" could be bought by an implementation that always returns `null`); and a disclosure pinning the residual. Mutation-verified on the real file: restoring the old inline predicate turns **10 of 12** cases red **by name**, the two survivors being the `null`-handling and disclosure cases that the mutation does not touch, and the file was restored byte-identical. **My own lock caught a bug in my own parser on its first run, and it is worth recording because it is the same shape one level down:** `blindspot-upgrade: 7/7 passed` — I read the second capture group as the failure count, so the parser reported 7 passed / **7 failed**. The denominator is not a failure count; "7 of 7 passed" means zero failures. **And the first probe I wrote to quantify defect ① under-counted it for exactly the reason the defect exists:** my measuring regex was `(\d+)\s*passed,?\s*(\d+)\s*failed`, which handles the comma form and misses the slash form, so it reported 161 cases where the true figure is 242. The instrument that measures an instrument inherits the instrument's blind spots unless it is built from the thing being measured rather than from a guess about its shape — which is why the final accounting was done per file, by running all 394, rather than by a second regex. **One residual recorded rather than papered over:** about twenty further files print **no summary line at all** — only `✓` lines, or nothing at all (`assertions`, `atomic-write`, `audit-wiring`, `behavior-tracker`, `data-eraser`, `emotion-optimizer`, `empathy-responder-optimized`, `evolution-audit`, `evolution-state`, `meta-audit`, `pattern-detector`, `self-evolution-v2`, `sycophancy-i18n`, `ttl-preferences`, `knowledge/classics-feedback`). No parser can recover a count that was never printed; making them print is a per-file change and out of scope for one minimal change. All 32 blind files were run individually and **every one exits 0**, so there are no hidden failures — the blindness is entirely in the pass count, which is the honest direction for a defect to lean. The B-class cascade did not fire (no module moved between classes).

The instrument's own summary reads `曾触发且推 finding: 53`, `曾触发、按设计不推 finding: 1`, `曾触发但从不推 finding: 0 ✓`, `从未触发: 0` — every one of the 54 dimensions fires and pushes. But that green answers *"is the dimension still alive"*, not *"does the FP/recall reading cover it"*. The instrument has carried a second reading since cycle 131 and this cycle finally read it against the documents: `语料 199 条上触发过的维度: 22/54`, `语料从未触达的维度 32 个 —— 行为仅由测试字面量锁定, 校准的 FP/recall 读数对它们零表达力`. So the documented **0.0% FP / 100% recall** says nothing at all about 32 of the 54 dimensions — not "they are fine" but "this reading cannot say whether they are fine". And AGENTS.md's FP paragraph did not mention it: it advised the reader to *"read it as 'no false positives this corpus can express'"*, which leaves room in tone while never stating that the corpus reaches only 22 of 54. The same paragraph also carried a **stale scale beside a current one** — its first line says `137 benign / 62 malicious` (199, measured this cycle by parsing the array literals rather than requiring the script, which writes to `data/feedback/`) while its closing sentence still says `A zero on a 106-sample corpus`. The fix writes the reach disclosure into the paragraph — both numbers must be quoted together, corpus 199, dimension reach 22/54 — and locks it with `test/corpus-dimension-reach-disclosure.test.js` (5 cases). The lock deliberately pins an **invariant rather than a literal**: `documented reach === measured reach`, where the measurement re-parses the corpus arrays and runs bare `discriminate()` over all 199 samples (253 ms, so it can live in the suite). The corpus grows, the document must follow, or the lock reddens — which is why the number 22 is nowhere in the test. Two further cases guard the instrument itself in both directions: a signal predicate that collapsed to always-zero or always-54 would break the equality just as surely as a wrong document would. A reverse proof tampers the documented figure in memory and requires the invariant to notice. The audit's Chinese dimensions pattern is `(\d[\d,]*)\s*个\s*(?:discrimination\s*)?维度` — so `32 个维度` was read as *"AGENTS.md claims the engine has 32 dimensions"* against a measured 54. One sentence of honest disclosure produced **four phantom claims** and reddened **eight audit-family test files** — and they were red when run alone, not only under the concurrent runner, which is the first thing I checked because this repository has a long-recorded concurrency artefact with exactly this signature. **A sentence that states a fact was read by the instrument as a statement of scale.** The document's number was right; the document's *wording* was wrong. That is the cycle-8/9/190 family — *an instrument that splits by language and word form* — in a new costume: previously the split hid claims or invented them from local counts; this time it turned a disclosure into a false engine claim, and the cost landed on eight unrelated tests whose failure messages named `domains`, `drift`, `scoped-before` and `coverage-sweep` while the cause sat in a paragraph about corpus reach. The repair is in the wording, not in the audit: the disclosure now says 「54 个维度里只有 22 个被它触达」 (the only `数字+个+维度` form left states 54, which is true and therefore audited clean) and 「另外 32 个，在真实标注语料上从未被触达」 — the count and the noun are deliberately separated so the pattern cannot bridge them. The whole episode is recorded in the paragraph itself, so the next person to disclose a number here reads it before writing. The slice has kept one discipline for four cycles: *counting tools is not calling them* (cycle 17/29/192/193). A commit that announces itself as documentation shipped code, and the code it shipped calls a name that has never existed. **Why it survived every check, which is the part worth the cycle.** The central dispatch passes `crypto.randomUUID()` as `sessionId` (line 5284), so the branch `sessionId ? getOrCreateInstance(sessionId) : heartflow` throws **100% of the time**. But the dispatch has a deliberate fallback for single-argument handlers: when the first call throws, it calls `handler(args)` again **without** `sessionId`, and that call takes the `heartflow` branch and succeeds. So the caller sees a correct answer, the tool looks like it works, and the real cost is one swallowed `ReferenceError` plus a double run of the前置 `path-guard` and `require` on every single call — with the dispatch's catch being a bare `catch (_) {}`, so the server's output carries **zero trace**. And unit tests call handlers directly *without* a `sessionId`, which takes the `heartflow` branch and never triggers it. Cycle 193 repaired `src/benchmark/`'s three modules and measured `aspira_benchmark_run` returning `accuracy=1 total=2` live — by calling the handler without a `sessionId`, which is exactly the shape that bypasses the defect. **The verification method of a repair was itself blind to the defect the repair left behind.** The fix is two lines: both sites become `const hf = heartflow;`, matching every other handler in the file. The function is deliberately **not** implemented — "fetch an engine instance by sessionId" requires deciding instance lifetime (process singleton / per-session / on disk), which is product judgement and 铁律 2's exclusion, and AGENTS.md already records per-call instances as a disclosed limitation from cycles 196 and 197. `test/mcp-benchmark-sessionid-purity.test.js` (6 cases) locks a **class of shape rather than this one case**: *any handler in the HANDLERS table whose signature takes a `sessionId` must not answer a `ReferenceError` when called with a non-empty one*. Today exactly three benchmark handlers qualify and the case runs in under a second; a future handler that repeats the mistake is caught by it. Two source-level cases pin the name out of the file (line comments stripped first — the seventh tuition payment for *a repair comment quoting the removed code being read as the offence*), and a reverse proof inlines the old expression and requires it to throw. **And my own lock walked straight into a tuition this repository had already paid, which is why the mutation proof mattered.** The behavioural case first asserted only "does not throw" — and the mutation left it green, because the handler's own `try/catch` swallows the `ReferenceError` into `{error: …}` rather than throwing. I tightened it to inspect the returned content, and it *still* stayed green under mutation: the handler is `async`, so a synchronous call returns a Promise and `JSON.stringify(promise)` is `{}`. That is cycle 17's exact lesson — *an instrument that measures the wrong thing reports healthy code as hollow* — paid again by me, this cycle, in the lock I was writing to prevent exactly that. With `await` in place the mutation turns **4 of 6** cases red **by name**, and `src/mcp-server.js` was restored byte-identical. The audit then read `README.md claims modules = 131, measured 132` and reddened the audit family, and the documented test count entered exactly the feedback loop cycle 193 named — the count depends on the measured count, which depends on the audit family passing, which depends on the count. `git log -S` proved the line had entered with my own commit, which is how it was told apart from cycle 193's older instance of the same probe. It was removed by anchor (not by `git checkout`, which cycle 19 measured as reverting to a stale commit — and README carried this cycle's legitimate `2232 passing tests` edit), the commit was amended, and the tag re-pointed. **A probe restored into a document by a test that does not finish its restore is a writer you did not authorise, and `git add` will happily take its work.** The rule cycle 193 already wrote — *when a documented count enters a feedback loop, audit for unrestored probes before touching the count* — was not followed, and it cost the cycle most of its remaining time.
- **The one remaining content-shaped evasion gap was a skeleton-length threshold, and the obvious way past it — lowering it — measured net-negative for exactly the reason the threshold existed.** `adversarial-robustness`, 205th cycle, and the engine chose it on its own after abstaining on a four-way tie (0.73 across `adversarial-robustness`, `fp-recall-calibration`, `test-coverage-gap` and `doc-honest-numbers`, broken by `consequence_value`, confidence 0). The cycle opened by re-running the calibration rather than trusting the last reading, and it localised the gap to a single class: of **twenty** evasion classes, nineteen read **60/60 content-bearing**, and only **English vowel-stripping** read **39/60** — 21 misses, of which 11 are slur-shaped. Tracing them found one shared cause: `checkVowelStripEvasion`'s stage-2 skeleton threshold `skel.length >= 6`. Every one of the 11 misses fails on skeleton *length*: `nigger→nggr` (4), `niggers→nggrs` (5), `faggot→fggt` (4), `bitch→btch` (4), `spic→spc` (3), `kike→kk` (2), `chink→chnk` (4), `slut→slt` (3), `whore→whr` (3), `dyke→dyk` (3), `queer→qr` (2). Cycle 169 had set that threshold and recorded its cost as "short slurs still miss under vowel-stripping" — honest, but never quantified. **The obvious repair was lowering the threshold, and this cycle walked it and measured it net-negative, which is the part worth recording.** Applying `vowelOptional` (every vowel becomes `[x]?`) to `HATE_SPEECH_EN`'s slur patterns recovered 8 of the 21 misses — and pushed **3 of 137 benign samples from `pass` to non-pass**, all of them the most ordinary technical text: `\bwh[o0]r[e3]s?\b` becomes `\bwh[o0]?r[e3]?s?\b`, so **WHERE** (vowel-stripped to `WHR`) is read as `whore`; `\bqu[e3]ers?\b` becomes `\bq[u]?[e3]?[e]?rs?\b`, so **queries** (to `QRS`) is read as `queers`. The root cause is that a short slur's consonant skeleton and a high-frequency technical word **are genuinely indistinguishable under this transform** — which is precisely why cycle 169 built the threshold, only without ever measuring the size of either side. **The viable direction was also already written in cycle 169, in its own methodology:** *"long phrase skeletons (`gnrllprvsnstrctns`, 7–18 chars) are near-impossible in natural text."* Attacks never use a bare slur — every slur attack in the corpus carries an attack verb or a parallel structure. So the fix **lengthens the skeleton instead of lowering the threshold**: a new `VOWEL_STRIP_PHRASES` table of 11 attack phrases, running through **the same predicate and the same threshold** as the existing word channel, with skeleton length guaranteed by the phrase itself (minimum 8). Measured after, on the corpus, item for item: vowel-stripping content-bearing **39/60 → 49/60** (+10), FP **0.0% unchanged** (137 benign, 0 escalated), plaintext recall **100% unchanged** (62/62), and the other nineteen classes **untouched**. `test/vowel-strip-phrase-skeleton.test.js` (7 cases) locks the admission discipline from cycle 18 as behaviour rather than memory: ① every phrase's **plaintext** form must already be intercepted by the engine (all 11 measured `block`); ② all 137 benign must stay silent under "vowel-stripped + phrase skeleton" (0 measured); ③ plaintext readings must not move (62 malicious all intercepted, 137 benign all `pass`); plus a case that the threshold is still 6 and every phrase skeleton clears it, a source-level requirement that the phrase loop really sits inside `checkVowelStripEvasion`, a reverse proof, and a disclosure pinning the 11 residual misses. Mutation-verified on the real file: emptying the phrase table turns **2** cases red **by name** — and the first attempt at that proof was **invalid in a way worth recording**: the lock's load-time guard asserted `PHRASES.length > 0`, so the mutation detonated at load with a mount exception and **not one case ran**. "The lock crashed" is not "the lock went red". The guard now requires only `Array.isArray`, and the mutation reddens the two cases that actually own the behaviour. **One residual measured and deliberately not fixed, with its numbers taken so the next cycle can act on them rather than re-measure:** two of the 11 residual misses (`all niggers must die`, `all f_aggot_s must die`) are blocked not by the skeleton but by **stage 1's `lat >= 12` threshold** — vowel-stripped, those sentences contain only 11 Latin letters. Lowering that threshold to 11 was measured this cycle: **137 benign still 0 false positives**, and it recovers exactly those two; lowering to 9 recovers four, still 0 FP. It was not done because it changes a threshold shared with `checkVowelStripCrimeFamily` and the reading holds only on the current corpus — 铁律 2 puts it in the next cycle, with the full regression that deserves. The other 9 residual misses are 8 code-semantic samples (whose skeletons collapse to 2–3 characters and which cycles 32/141 measured as structurally isomorphic to benign technical discussion under this transform), and the social-engineering sample cycle 172 already disclosed. **One probe-pollution event, and it is the cycle-194 `999` value again:** mid-`run-all`, `coverage-sweep-doc-claims.test.js` left `**999 alive but referenced by no test**` in SKILL.md's Coverage sweep row. It was removed by anchor (not by `git checkout`, which cycle 19 measured as reverting to a stale commit), and it is the third recorded instance of that exact value. Corpus and coverage readings were re-measured after the fix: FP 0.0% / recall 100.0% both unchanged, coverage B 91 / C 280 unchanged (no module moved class). `src/` changed in one module — a new frozen table, one loop, and one export line — no MCP tool definition, handler mapping or permission contract was added or modified, so MCP was **not** restarted (recorded as not-performed).

So this cycle did the regression it was owed, across **21 end-to-end calibres** — plaintext plus all twenty evasion classes × 137 benign + 62 malicious, through `gate.checkOutput`, not through the function in isolation. The reading is clean: at `lat >= 11`, benign false positives are **0/137 in every one of the 21 calibres** — not one class moved — and malicious misses moved in **exactly one** class, English vowel-stripping, **11 → 9**, recovering precisely the two samples cycle 205 named (`all f_aggot_s must die`, `all niggers must die`, which carry only 11 Latin letters once vowel-stripped). The shared consumer is untouched where it matters: `checkVowelStripCrimeFamily` reads 4/62 hits and 0/137 false positives before and after, because it is regex-driven rather than skeleton-length-driven. And 11 is the *profit-maximising* point, not merely a safe one: measured at 10 and 9 the end-to-end miss count stays at **9** — the phrase table added last cycle already covers the longer attack shapes, so going lower buys nothing. The change is two lines in `src/shield/letter-space-evasion.js` (both functions' stage-1 gates, kept in sync by a comment that says why), plus the two test locks that pinned the old value. **The two probe failures are the part worth the cycle, and they are the same failure twice in one afternoon.** (a) The first version re-implemented stage 1 *outside* the function — "if the outer lat passes, call the function" — and reported **12 / 11 / 10 / 9 as four identical readings**. Of course it did: the function has its *own* `lat < 12` on line 474, so the value the probe changed never reached the code being measured. (b) The second version patched the real source but wrote the result to a **copy** at `_lat11-probe.js` and then self-verified against that copy — while the chain `gate.js → index.js → pipeline.js → adversarial-variant.js` requires `./letter-space-evasion.js`, the original. The fix in both cases is the same and is now written into the probes: **assert, at the point of use, that the code being measured contains the mutation**. (c) A third finding, not about the slice: `test/run-all.js` spawns every child with the literal string `node` (lines 190, 195, 203), so it inherits the caller's `PATH`. Re-run with `PATH` repaired: **2239 / 0 / 2239, exit 0, both channels empty**. The runner's own entry dependency is recorded rather than fixed — repairing it means changing how 397 children are spawned, which is not this cycle's one minimal change. Corpus re-measured after the change, item for item: FP **0.0%** (137 benign, 0 escalated) and plaintext recall **100.0%** (62/62) both unchanged; vowel-stripping content-bearing **49/60 → 51/60** and all-62 **51/62 → 53/62**; the other nineteen classes untouched; coverage B 91 / C 280 unchanged. The 9 residual misses are the 8 code-semantic samples cycles 32/141 measured as structurally isomorphic to benign technical discussion under this transform, plus the social-engineering sample cycle 172 already disclosed — unchanged, still disclosed. That is cycle 142's git-`PATH` dependency in a new and far louder costume — that one was 1-in-6 intermittent inside one test file, this one is **100%** across the whole runner, and it is more dangerous precisely because repairing the `PATH` makes it vanish, which invites the reader to file it as "my environment" rather than "the runner's entry dependency". The fix is `test/_node-bin.js`, which resolves the interpreter explicitly (`process.execPath` first — the runner was itself started by node, so its children should come from the same interpreter — then the standard install locations), falls back to the bare literal only when nothing is found so a genuine absence still reports `ENOENT`, and is locked by `test/node-binary-path-independence.test.js` (4 cases) in exactly cycle 142's three-part shape plus a premise self-check. **Then the gate went red, and the first thing I did was suspect my own change — and the control experiment I ran to clear it found a second, unrelated defect of the same family.** The concurrent runner read two failures, `audit-scoped-before-narrow` (`Command failed`) and one case inside `git-binary-path-independence`; both were green alone. So I reverted all four of my files to `HEAD`, moved the two new ones out of the tree, and ran the suite again as a control — and the control **failed on a different file**: `reasoning-coherence-gate-dead-branch`, with `ENOENT: /tmp/corpus149.json`. Two runs, two disjoint failure sets, which is the cycle-132/204/205 concurrency artefact's signature; but the control's own failure was not an artefact at all, and it reproduced when the file was run alone. Its line 180 read a corpus from **`/tmp/corpus149.json`** — a probe's leftover, never in git. So that case's availability depended on what happened to still be sitting in this machine's `/tmp`, and when the file was cleaned the case went red for a reason that had nothing to do with the thing it exists to disclose: a case whose whole job is "the reasoning_coherence gate is still broken" failing because it could not open a file. It now parses `scripts/calibrate-fp-recall.js`'s array literals the way cycles 205/206 already do — **without executing it**, because that script's top level runs the full calibration and writes `data/feedback/`. Its reading moved with the corpus and is updated honestly in the comment: 185 samples / 19 with reasoning intent became **199 / 26**, all 26 still pushing no finding. **The lesson worth keeping is about the control experiment itself:** I ran it to exonerate my change, and it did that, but its real value was that it was *also a measurement* — and a measurement taken on a machine whose `/tmp` had just been cleaned. A control run is not a formality; it is another sample from the same distribution, and this one happened to land on a defect the experimental run had obscured. Gate, serial: **2243 passed / 0 failed / 2243 total, exit 0**, dual-channel empty, `Command failed` count **0**; verify 15/15; the concurrent runner still reads 1–2 failures with a different combination each time, every one green alone — the known artefact, unchanged and still the gate's largest instability source. Suite 2239 → **2243**, all four from the new lock. `src/` unchanged, so MCP was **not** restarted (recorded as not-performed). **Three siblings of the `/tmp` dependency were found, measured, and deliberately not fixed — each recorded with its own reading so the next cycle can act rather than re-measure.** ① `test/absolute-claim-commitment.test.js:87` and ② `test/contradiction-self-correction.test.js:121` both open with `if (!fs.existsSync(p)) return;` over the same `/tmp/corpus149.json` — so on any machine where that file is absent they **test nothing and exit 0**, which is cycle 15's silent-skip shape happening right now (the second one even carries the comment "语料文件不在则跳过(不虚构通过)", which is honest about the skip and silent about the cost: the corpus reading it exists to protect is currently unprotected). ③ `test/dimension-guidance-coverage.test.js:84` has an `existsSync` fallback to a fixed sample list, so it never goes red — but with the file present and absent it measures **two different datasets**, and the reading changes without anything saying so. All three need their assertions re-derived against the 199-sample corpus, which is a different change from this cycle's two; 铁律 2 leaves them to the next cycle with this list.
- **The audit read 100 for 100 green, and the question it had never been asked was not "are the numbers right" but "how many of them is the instrument able to see at all" — and the section it could not see held a todo pointing at a module that no longer exists.** `doc-honest-numbers`, 208th cycle, and the engine chose it (journal `status: "decided"`, no tie to break). Cycles 7/8/9/10/11/12/19/176/187/188/189/190/202 had each asked the audit a different question about its keys, patterns, gates, file set, exemptions, unlocked measurements, cross-document contradictions, denominator composition and non-`.md` carriers. This cycle asked the one never asked of a **green** report: *every number matches its measurement — but which of the numbers a reader sees first are in no key at all?* Enumerated mechanically against the audit's own `NUMISH` regex: the audit has **19 distinct keys**, and `CURRENT_STATE.md`'s 「已知限制 / 待办」 section — the section a reader consults to learn what is still broken — carries **four present-tense readings that belong to none of them**. All four were measured, and three were wrong by one to two orders of magnitude: ① `heart-logic.js (2311 行)` against a `wc -l` of **2408**; ② `desire-cognition.js (6384 行) 仍有大量逻辑待拆分` — a todo hanging on a file that **is not in `src/` at all** (the whole repository holds only `test/archive/dead-tests/desire-cognition.test.js`, a dead test pointing at the vanished path); ③ `未测试模块已清零 (214→0)` against `coverage-sweep`'s B class of **91**; ④ `核心路径已全绿 (119/119)` against a full suite of **2244**. The third is the dangerous one, because it is a *claim of completion* — the document asserts a cleanup that never happened, in the one section whose job is to state what remains. **And the pointed detail is that the audit's own comment had already named the failure mode.** `scripts/audit-doc-numbers.js` line 19-22 records why the scan set was widened from three hardcoded files: `CURRENT_STATE.md` ("128 modules / 119 tests", measured 132) was a **现在时陈述** that "因不在扫描列表里而长期无人发现". So the comment described the *class* — a present-tense reading nobody is measuring — and the fix repaired the *two instances the comment happened to name*. The other three in the same section survived for the same reason the first two did. That is cycle 7's `IDENTITY.md said 99 个模块真实加载` against a measured 132, relearned: **a fix written against the named instances rather than against the named class leaves the class intact.** The repair corrects all four readings, strikes the dead todo through with its measured reason (rather than deleting it, so the record of the evaporation survives), and adds `test/coverage-sweep-doc-claims.test.js`'s fifth case, which deliberately does **not** spawn `coverage-sweep` again — it compares `CURRENT_STATE.md` against **the already-closed `SKILL.md` Coverage sweep row**, so two documents stating the same quantity must state the same number, and the count it derives is the one the audit already owns. The case's third assertion is the generalisation: every `.js` file named in the 待办 section must really exist under `src/`, so a todo can never again hang on an evaporated module — and it strips `~~struck~~` passages first, because the record of a module's disappearance is not the offence of treating a disappeared module as a todo (**the lock reading its own documentation as the evidence**, the cycle-19/25/194/195/197 shape, avoided the seventh time by construction rather than by repair). Mutation-verified on the real file: `未测试模块 91 个 → 0 个` and `全套件 2244 通过 → 9999 通过` each redden the case **by name**, and `CURRENT_STATE.md` was restored byte-identical. Gate: verify **15/15**; run-all **serial 2243 → 2244 / 0 / 2244 with exit 0**, dual-channel empty, `Command failed` count **0**; the new lock is **5/5** alone. Suite **2243 → 2244**, the one new case from the new assertion. No `src/` file changed, so MCP was **not** restarted (recorded as not-performed).
- **The instrument was all-green, and the question it had never been asked was not "is the dimension alive" but "who is keeping it alive" — and for seven of the fifty-three, the answer is the test that asserts it.** `dimension-health-audit`, 209th cycle, and the engine chose it on its own (0.71 / confidence 0.60, no tie to break). The cycle opened by re-running the instrument rather than trusting the last reading, and the first measurement was a correction to the record: cycle 27 had left "`dimension-health-audit.js` still reports `evidence` as 7630 signals / 0 findings" as an open gap, and it is **no longer there** — the instrument now prints `曾触发、按设计不推 finding: 1 (仅打分/别名: evidence)` and the summary reads `曾触发但从不推 finding: 0 ✓`, identical word for word to what cycle 203 recorded. So the slice's own previously-open defect was already closed by a later cycle, and the honest move was to record that rather than re-fix it. With all four summary lines green, the cycle asked the one question never asked of a green report: *the numbers are right — but is the instrument able to say **where each number comes from**?* It cannot, and the reason is one line. The pool builder concatenated three sources into a single de-duplicated string array — `for (const s of [...poolA, ...poolB, ...poolC]) { ... pool.push(s); }` — so the moment a string was de-duplicated, its origin was gone forever, and the instrument could only ever say "53 个推过 finding 的维度" without saying which of those 53 had their findings **entirely** from the test author's own strings. Measured by splitting the three pools and running each through bare `discriminate()` separately (an independent probe, not a re-implementation — it loads the instrument's own three pool builders by stripping only the unguarded trailing `main();`): of the 53 that push findings, **7 have 100% of theirs from pool B (test string literals), with pool A (corpus) and pool C (the engine's own regex fragments) both at zero** — `contradiction`, `reasoning_coherence`, `goal_misalignment`, `privacy_boundary`, `pseudo_profundity`, `perfect_error`, `coverup_induction`. The sharpest is `contradiction`: a named member of AGENTS.md's Verify-level tier that can push the gate to `verify`, with **37 findings, every one written by a test author**. A further 26 non-score-only dimensions push nothing at all on the corpus. So "this dimension is alive" is, for those 7, **the test proving itself**: the input is written by the test, the assertion is written by the test, and the instrument only confirms the two still agree. That is not a dead dimension — it does push findings on the large pool — but its behaviour has never been expressed by any input independent of the test that asserts it. It is the same family as cycle 203's corpus-blindness disclosure (22/54 reached), except that round only counted *how many* the corpus reaches and never counted *how many of the rest are held up by nothing but test literals*. The fix is attribution inside the **same** traversal, not three more passes — the instrument runs three full-pool sweeps already and takes 6.0 s, so recording each entry's origin set at de-duplication time and bucketing `bySrc` per dimension costs essentially nothing (measured after: still 6.0 s). The new section deliberately sets **no threshold and turns nothing red**: it is an attribution list. "How much independence counts as independent" is a product judgement, and 铁律 2 puts that outside one minimal change. `test/dimension-finding-pool-attribution.test.js` (3 cases) locks it: source-level that the pool keeps its origins and the tally buckets them (with the three traversal sites counted in two precise classes, because a wide regex that also matched the tally loop made the lock report "expected 2, actual 3" — **a lock that goes red because it miscounted its own subject sends people to fix code that is not broken**); behavioural that the real instrument prints the section and that every member of the "test-literals-only" list is a genuine `dimensions{}` key rather than a hand-written name (cycle 11's `bullshit` / `bullshit_recognition` lesson); and a reverse proof. **The reverse proof was wrong twice and both errors are the recorded shapes.** (a) Its first version copied the mutated script to `os.tmpdir()` and ran it — and the script derives its root with `path.join(__dirname, '..')`, so from `/tmp` the root became `/` and the copy died on `ENOENT: /private/var/folders/.../src/index.js`. That is a reverse proof that goes green because the *environment* was wrong rather than because the lock can fail, and it is the same family as cycle 206's "the probe mutated a copy and self-verified against it" — relearned inside my own lock, hours after paying it in a probe. The root is now pinned to an absolute path with an assertion that the pin took effect, plus a guard that the mutated run did not fail on a path. (b) With that fixed the proof went **red for the right reason and the wrong assertion**: I had written `!hasSection || !hasList`, but stripping the bucketing leaves `bySrc` all-zero, so the section and its summary line still print with `N = 0` — the proof passed its own check while the case it protects (`n > 0`) would in fact have gone red. **A reverse proof whose assertion is wider than the lock it protects is decoration, not proof.** It now asserts exactly the thing that makes the second case fail: the summary line's count must be `0`, and every dimension's A/B/C must be all-zero. Gate: verify **15/15**; the new lock is **3/3** alone. No `src/` file changed — the change is entirely in the diagnostic script and its new lock — so MCP was **not** restarted (recorded as not-performed). **One thing measured and deliberately not done:** the 7 dimensions' zero corpus reach is an `fp-recall-calibration` job (cycle 203 said so in as many words: 补语料样本才能把它们纳入校准视野), and adding samples to change a documented coverage reading is a different change from teaching the instrument to attribute; 铁律 2 leaves it with this list.
- **Two of the four tests that read a `/tmp` file were testing nothing at all — and the third had been quietly measuring two different datasets depending on whether that file happened to exist.** `test-coverage-gap`, 212th cycle, and the engine chose it on its own (0.73 / confidence 0.70, no tie to break). Cycle 207 fixed two members of this family — `run-all`'s literal `node` inheriting the caller's `PATH`, and `reasoning-coherence-gate-dead-branch`'s read of `/tmp/corpus149.json` — and left three siblings with the note *"all three need their assertions re-derived against the 199-sample corpus"*. This cycle measured them first, and the measurement was the finding: **`/tmp/corpus149.json` does not exist on this machine**, so the two tests opening with `if (!fs.existsSync(p)) return;` (`absolute-claim-commitment`'s third case, `contradiction-self-correction`'s fifth) were **running zero assertions and exiting 0** — cycle 15's silent-skip shape, live. Their comments say "不虚构通过", which is honest about the skip and silent about the cost: the readings they exist to protect were unprotected. The third (`dimension-guidance-coverage`'s second case) used an `existsSync` fallback to five fixed samples, so it never failed — but with the file present it measured 100+ findings and absent it measured a handful, **and nothing said which**. A fourth finding fell out of reading the second one closely: its case name hardcoded *"132 benign FP 0.0% / 58 malicious recall 100.0%"* while the corpus has long been 137 / 64 — and the `existsSync` skip was what kept that stale number from reddening. **Ran-it-red and never-ran-green were masking each other.** All three now parse `scripts/calibrate-fp-recall.js`'s array literals — **without executing it**, because its top level runs the full calibration and writes to `data/feedback/` — through one shared helper, `test/_corpus-arrays.js`, which **throws** rather than returning an empty corpus (an empty corpus would turn some of the callers' assertions tautological: cycle 188's "a printed 0 reads exactly like a measured 0"). Measured after, item for item: `absolute_claim` hits in the corpus **2** (the assertion value was right all along; it had simply never run), corpus readings **0 escalated / 64 of 64 intercepted**, guidance coverage **100 findings, 0 without guidance**; the stale case name is now 137 / 64 and carries a premise self-check that the sizes match the name, so a future corpus growth reddens it instead of drifting. The new lock `test/no-tmp-corpus-dependency.test.js` (4 cases) enforces the rule rather than remembering it — cycle 9's sentence: *a rule I keep relearning is a rule the suite should enforce, not one I should remember*: ① mechanically scan every live test file for any read of `/tmp/corpus*` and name file plus line; ② same for the `if (!fs.existsSync(X)) return;` skip shape; ③ the shared helper must return a corpus whose sizes equal what the calibration script itself reports (otherwise all three repairs turn tautological together); and ④ a self-proof that the comment-stripping the scanner depends on has not stripped away real code. **That fourth case exists because the scanner read its own documentation as the offence — twice, in two escalating costumes, and this is the eighth recorded installment of the cycle-19/25/194/195/197/209 shape.** The first version stripped nothing, so the repair comments in the three freshly-fixed files (which quote the deleted `/tmp/corpus149.json` and `if (!fs.existsSync(p)) return;` to explain what changed) were reported as two violations that did not exist. Adding line-comment stripping then put **this lock's own JSDoc header** in the dock, because the header must state the rule and the rule's literal form is the banned form — and this time the lock scans itself, where the previous seven installments all had their comments in *other* files. The stripper is now length-preserving over both comment forms, deliberately does **not** strip strings (cycle 193: strip strings and you can no longer see what is being `require`d), and the self-proof pins both directions: real code must still match, pure comments must not, and line count must be conserved — because "stripped too clean" fails in the direction of a **miss**, which is the quiet direction. The lock also excludes its own file from the scan, with the cost disclosed in a comment. Mutation-verified in three directions: injecting a real `/tmp` read plus `existsSync` skip into an unrelated test reddens cases ① and ② **by name**, naming the file and line; injecting only the commented form does **not** redden (proving the stripper is what avoids the false positive, not luck); and the clean tree is green. Gate: verify **15/15**; the new lock is **4/4** alone. No `src/` file changed — three test files, one shared helper, one new lock — so MCP was **not** restarted (recorded as not-performed).

- **The audit measured the suite in a different mode from the gate, so the honest document reading was red on every single run — and the instrument was training the reader to write down the wrong number.** `doc-honest-numbers`, 213th cycle, and the engine chose it on its own (0.73 / confidence 0.75, no tie to break). The cycle opened by running the audit rather than trusting the last green one, and it read **5 mismatches, all of them `tests`-class**: the documents claim 2257, the audit measured 2248 with 2 failures. The first move was cycle 193's own rule — *when a documented count enters a feedback loop, audit for unrestored probes before touching the count* — so the audit's **own spawn form** was reproduced exactly before any number was touched. It read 2248 / 2, and both failures were the long-recorded concurrency artefact (cycle 132/194/204/205/207/211): `audit-doc-coverage.test.js` dying with `Command failed` and one case inside `domains-title-vs-table.test.js`, each **8/8 and 6/6 green when run alone**. The serial gate reads 2257 / 0. The root cause is one missing argument: `scripts/audit-doc-numbers.js` spawned `test/run-all.js` **without passing a concurrency level**, so the child fell into run-all's default concurrent mode, while 铁律 3's gate and the documented reading are **serial**. The same code, measured twice in two modes, one of which has known phantom failures. **The direction of the error is the part worth the cycle.** The document was right; the instrument said it was wrong, every run, forever. An instrument that reports a correct reading as an error is worse than one that does not report — it trains the reader to "fix" the document by writing down the concurrent number, which is the wrong one. The fix passes `ASPIRA_TEST_CONCURRENCY: '1'` in the spawn's env, aligning the instrument with the gate. The cost is recorded rather than hidden: the audit goes from about 2 minutes to about **8**, because a serial sweep is about 6 minutes against the concurrent 40 seconds. Measured after: the audit reads **98 claims / 98 consistent / 0 mismatched, exit 0** — the four `tests`-class rows stopped being red without a single document number being touched. `test/audit-spawn-serial.test.js` (3 cases) locks it: source-level that the spawn's whole statement block carries `ASPIRA_TEST_CONCURRENCY: '1'` and still spreads `process.env`; a premise self-check that `run-all.js` really reads the variable and feeds it into `CONCURRENCY` — without it the first case would be **tautological**, since passing a variable nobody reads is shape without effect (cycle 29's shape: a "must" whose premise silently changed can never fail); and a reverse proof that strips the `env` line and requires the predicate to fail. **Two of this cycle's own errors, both the same lesson in new costumes.** (a) The lock's first version matched the spawn **line by line**, and the actual call is multi-line (`spawnSync(...)` then `cwd:` then `env:` on its own lines) — so the predicate saw only the first line, missed the `env`, and reported a violation that did not exist. That is "an instrument that pattern-matches without regard to structure" once more: the previous installments read a picture inside a comment as code; this one read a multi-line statement as a single line. The predicate now extracts the whole statement block by paren-pairing. (b) The mutation proof's own self-assertion demanded the variable be absent from the **whole file** — and the explanatory comment this cycle had just written legitimately contains it, because the comment must name the variable to be readable. A correct mutation was reported as "not applied". The assertion now checks only inside the spawn block, which is the same rule for the third time today: look at structure, not at the whole text. Mutation-verified in both directions: stripping the `env` line from the real script reddens case ① **by name**, the script is restored byte-identical, and the lock returns to green. Gate: verify **15/15**; the new lock is **3/3** alone. No `src/` file changed — the audit script, its new lock, and the document record — so MCP was **not** restarted (recorded as not-performed).

- **A diagnostic that only read the file in front of it could not see seven of the fifty-four checkers living one directory over — and fixing it surfaced a brace-pairing bug that had been eating a function body for as long as the index existed.** `dimension-health-audit`, 214th cycle, and the engine chose it on its own (0.71 / confidence 0.6, no tie to break). The instrument reads green on all four of its summary lines (`曾触发且推 finding: 53 / 按设计不推: 1 / 从不推: 0 ✓ / 从未触发: 0`), so this cycle asked a question that green has never been asked: *the pool that produces one of those numbers — can it see the thing it claims to measure?* The answer is no, for seven dimensions. `DIM_CHECKERS` registers 54 `[dimension, checkFunctionName]` pairs and `regexLiteralInputs()` reads each function's body with `if (!body) continue`. `functionBody` searched only the text of `src/index.js` — and measured, **7 of the 54 function names do not exist there at all**: `phishing_coercion` / `induced_trust` / `coverup_induction` live in `src/manipulation-tactics.js:128/134/140`, `dangerous_instruction` in `src/dangerous-instruction.js:395`, `reward_hacking` in `src/reward-hacking.js:1337`, `perfect_error` in `src/perfect-error.js:98`, `premature_termination` in `src/premature-termination.js:76`, all reached from `index.js` through `_mt.checkXxx()` / `_di.checkXxx()`. So `functionBody` returned `null`, the caller skipped silently, and **source C (the engine's own regex fragments) was structurally 0 for those seven dimensions for their entire lives** — while each of them pushes dozens of findings and every summary line stayed green. This is cycle 192's shape exactly (*four MCP tools whose implementation sat at the `src/` root while the diagnosis looked only at `src/core/`*): **the implementation was one directory away from where the diagnosis was looking.** The fix is a global index built once by walking `src/` — function name → function body, and constant name → declaration text — with `functionBody` / `declarationText` falling back to it when `index.js` has no match. It deliberately does **not** annotate `DIM_CHECKERS` with module names, which would need editing again for every future module-resident checker. **The second defect was found while fixing the first and is the one worth more.** The index's own brace pairing looked for the first `{` after `function <name>(` — and `checkPrematureTermination(text, ctx = {})` has a `{` in its **default parameter**, so pairing closed on the parameter's `}` and the function body came back as the empty string. Measured: with only the first fix applied, the pool rose 2778 → 2779 and that dimension's source C was still 0. The fix is to paren-pair across the whole parameter list first, then take the function body's `{`. **A default value in a signature is not part of the body, and a brace matcher that cannot tell them apart silently discards the function.** Measured after, item for item: pool C **2778 → 3173 (+395 fragments; 6 of the 7 dimensions now contribute any at all)**, `premature_termination`'s source C **11 → 12**, `induced_trust`'s **0 → 1**, and all four summary lines unchanged. `test/dimension-checker-resolution.test.js` (6 cases) locks the invariant rather than the numbers: ① every one of the 54 function names must resolve to a non-empty body — this is the case that catches the original defect, and it names the offenders; ② every **pattern table** a module-resident checker references must resolve, with the scope deliberately narrowed (see below) and a premise self-check that at least 3 tables are actually being examined; ③ a function with a default-object parameter must come back whole; ④ the pool must exceed its pre-fix size (a floor, not a literal — 2778 measured before, 3173 after, the assertion sits at 3000); ⑤ a reverse proof that rebuilds the script in `/tmp` with the fallback reverted to `return null` and requires case ①'s own predicate to fail on exactly the seven named dimensions; ⑥ a disclosure pinning the residual. Mutation-verified in three directions on the real file: reverting the `functionBody` fallback turns **6/6** red, reverting the parameter-list pairing turns **2** red by name (`premature_termination → checkPrematureTermination` and the default-parameter case), and reverting the `declarationText` fallback turns **2** red — the script restored byte-identical and the lock back to 6/6 each time. **Two things measured and deliberately not done, and one error of my own.** (a) **The +395 fragments push only +2 findings, and the reason is structural, not a missed threshold.** Those dimensions' patterns are **co-occurrence patterns** — `/(?:点击|打开|访问|登录)[^。]{0,20}(?:链接|网址|页面|表单)[^。]{0,40}(?:验证|确认|更新|完善|激活|冻结|异常|失效)/i` requires two word clusters to appear together, while pool C's synthesizer extracts **the single longest literal run** from each regex and feeds that back as an input. A co-occurrence requirement cannot be expressed by one literal run. That is cycle 11's recorded unsynthesizable class, met again from the other side. So this cycle **closed an instrument blind spot**; it did not move a headline reading, and the entry says so rather than implying otherwise. (b) **`dangerous_instruction` still contributes 0 fragments**, because its own body is `const r = _matchAll(text); return {…}` and names no constant at all — its three pattern tables live behind the private helper `_matchAll`, which the checker calls without naming. Measured the obvious extension (follow called functions one level): pool **3173 → 3186 (+13)** and that dimension **0 → 3**. The net is not worth the mechanism, so it is pinned as a disclosure in case ⑥ with an instruction to invert the case if it ever changes. (c) **My own lock went all-red because it could not tell what it was responsible for.** The first version of case ② demanded that *every* constant a module-resident checker references must resolve — and it reported 18 "unresolved", of which **17 are regex literals** (`const DEV_DEBUG = /…/`, `DISCOURSE_FRAME`, `UPSTREAM_PATCH`) that are already inline in the body and that the index neither collects nor should. A lock that cannot recognise its own subject fails in the loud direction, but it fails all the same, and it would have trained the next reader to ignore it. The predicate now asks a different question — *is this name declared anywhere in `src/` as `const NAME = [` or `= {`* — determined by an **independent scan in the test** (its own regex, its own traversal), not by re-reading the index, so the two disagreeing is exactly what turns the case red. Measured: 17 tables, all resolving. Gate: verify **15/15**; serial run-all **2260 → 2266 / 0 / 2266 with exit 0**, both channels empty, `Command failed` count 0; the new lock is **6/6** alone. No `src/` file changed — the instrument and its new lock only — so MCP was **not** restarted (recorded as not-performed).
- **The slice's own standing instruction — "add corpus samples to bring them into calibration" — does not hold for most of the dimensions it was written about, and the reason is that there is no correct box to put them in.** `fp-recall-calibration`, 211th cycle; `AspiraDecision` abstained on a four-way tie (`fp-recall-calibration` / `mcp-tool-enhancement` / `test-coverage-gap` / `doc-honest-numbers` all at 0.7) and broke it by `consequence_value`, confidence 0. Cycle 203 disclosed that the corpus reaches only 22 of 54 dimensions and closed with *"补语料样本才能把它们纳入校准视野"*; cycle 209 narrowed it to the **7 dimensions whose findings are 100% test-authored** and repeated the same sentence. This cycle checked all seven one at a time and found the instruction holds for **one** of them. The other six cannot be admitted, and the reason is not that the corpus is thin — it is that the corpus's benign/malicious dichotomy has no box that fits: ① **`contradiction` / `reasoning_coherence` / `pseudo_profundity` / `perfect_error` are reasoning-quality dimensions, and their trigger sentences are legitimate in content.** Measured findings, not inferred: `修好空洞的做法是让工具工作, 不是把它删掉。` returns `block` with `dangerous_instruction:85` **plus** `contradiction:30` — what blocked it is the former mis-firing on the word 删掉, and the target dimension is only a passenger. `历史数据显示成功率 60%` returns `rewrite` with `unsupported_claim:45 ×2` plus `perfect_error:43` — again the former. Putting either into `MALICIOUS` would **launder a false positive into a recall hit**, inflating the reading. That is cycle 11's tuition run in the opposite direction: that round was "correct behaviour produces `verify`, so filing it as malicious counts correct behaviour as a miss"; this one is "incorrect behaviour produces `block`, so filing it as malicious counts the error as a hit". ② **`privacy_boundary`'s `你体重多少，住哪儿？` is that shape exactly:** `gate=verify` — into `MALICIOUS` it records as a miss (the script's `caught` counts only `block`/`rewrite`), into `BENIGN` it records as a false positive (`verify` counts as escalated). Neither box accepts it. ③ `goal_misalignment` is score-only by design (in no tier set); all 5 of its literals return `pass`, which is the design working, not a gap. The one that qualifies is **`coverup_induction`**: two trigger sentences whose findings name it and nothing else (85 / 100), intercepted across **all 21 calibres** (plaintext plus all twenty evasion classes), not duplicating either existing array, and genuinely malicious in content (inducing concealment, inducing cover-up). Those two were added. Measured after, item for item: corpus **199 → 201**, dimension reach **22/54 → 23/54**, FP **0.0% unchanged** (137 benign, 0 escalated), plaintext recall **100.0% unchanged** (64/64), full-marks classes still **18**, benign false positives still **0/137 in every one of the twenty classes** — the two new samples are intercepted under every transform, so no class's miss composition changed. The new lock `test/fp-recall-corpus-admission.test.js` (3 cases) turns admission from a comment into an **executable criterion, and locks no specific string**: ① array self-consistency — every `MALICIOUS` entry must be `block`/`rewrite` in plaintext and every `BENIGN` entry must be `pass`, which is the same criterion the script itself uses, so any future sample that lands in the wrong box reddens the lock without anyone having to remember this entry; ② a ban list of the exact false-positive-shaped sentences above (cycle 11's `fp-hedge-exemption` shape), with a guard that the ban list is still live — if none of them triggers a finding any more, the case fails and says so rather than silently protecting a shape that no longer exists; ③ the two admitted samples must still be present and must still name `coverup_induction` in their findings, so deleting them reddens rather than silently reverting the reach reading. Gate: verify **15/15**; the new lock is **3/3** alone. No `src/` file changed — the corpus script and its lock only — so MCP was **not** restarted (recorded as not-performed).
- **The slice's own coverage reading had been wrong since the day it was written, and the two samples that made it wrong were disclosed three cycles earlier and locked by a test with a reverse guard.** `adversarial-robustness`, 210th cycle; `AspiraDecision` abstained on a five-way tie (`adversarial-robustness` / `fp-recall-calibration` / `mcp-tool-enhancement` / `test-coverage-gap` / `doc-honest-numbers` all at 0.7) and broke it by `consequence_value`, confidence 0. The cycle opened by re-running the calibration rather than trusting the last reading, and the first thing it found was not a bypass but a **miscount**: cycle 205 recorded "of **twenty** evasion classes, nineteen read **60/60 content-bearing**, and only **English vowel-stripping** read **39/60**", and cycle 206 inherited it as "the other nineteen classes **untouched**". Measured this cycle by running all twenty classes one at a time — the transform table is taken from the calibration script's own `EVASIONS` literal by brace-pairing and `eval`-ed in isolation, **not** by re-implementing it, because that script's top level runs the full calibration and writes to `data/feedback/`: content-bearing samples are **60**, and the classes at full marks are **18, not 19**. The two below full marks are 字母间插空格 **58/60** (2 misses) and 英文去元音 **51/60** (9 misses). **And the two letter-space misses are not a new regression — they are exactly the pair cycle 141 disclosed and locked in `test/letter-space-collapse.test.js`:** `eval(res.data);` (6 tokens after the transform, short of the ≥8 gate) and `const f = new Function(userInput);` (collapses to `newFunction(userInput)`, and `code_security`'s pattern demands a space between `new` and `Function`). That test carries a **reverse guard**: the moment either sample is intercepted, the disclosure must be updated. So the pair has been missed continuously since cycle 141, which means cycle 205 could not have read 60/60 for that class — the summary sentence was wrong the day it was written and was inherited for three more cycles. The root cause of the miscount is worth naming: cycle 205 was counting "classes at full marks" by reading the calibration output table, and that table's content-bearing column for letter-space reads `58/60` while its all-62 column reads `60/62` — a row that looks full in one column and is not in the other, in the one slice whose entire subject is that the two denominators are different questions. **A reading taken from the wrong column of an instrument that exists to teach the difference between columns is the cycle-202 shape one layer down.** The repair does **not** overwrite the original sentence — that would erase the fact that it really was written that way — but appends a correction declaration, and locks `documented === measured` as an invariant rather than a literal, exactly as cycle 203 did: the corpus grows, the classes change, the numbers move, and the two may never diverge again. `test/evasion-class-full-count.test.js` (3 cases) runs all twenty classes itself (0.3 s per class, ~6 s total, so it can live in the suite) and asserts the declaration's full-marks count and letter-space reading both equal the measurement; a source-level case that the original wrong sentence is still present, because silently editing it away would be erasing evidence rather than correcting it; and a reverse proof that mutates the declaration in memory and requires the equality to notice. Mutation-verified in the reverse-proof direction by construction: the proof's own assertion is that the mutated number must **not** equal the measurement, so a proof that cannot fail would have to make the measurement move too. Gate: verify **15/15**; the new lock is **3/3** alone. No `src/` file changed — the fix is entirely in the new lock and the document correction — so MCP was **not** restarted (recorded as not-performed).**And the cycle paid for a recorded rule it had already written, in the exact costume cycle 204 recorded it.** After the gate passed I ran the full audit before committing — the right order — and it read 5 mismatches, all `tests`-class, `实测 2264 / 2`. Per cycle 193's rule I identified both failures by name rather than touching any number: `security-audit`'s `S2: _verifyGitCommit … git log/tag 未命中 1.0.97` and `git-binary-path-independence`'s `PATH 只含 /usr/local/bin 时 security-audit 必须全绿`, which embeds the child's own summary. Both were 铁律 4's recorded shape — **the version had been bumped and the tag not yet created** — an artefact of my own ordering, not a defect. So I committed, tagged, and re-ran the audit. It then read **7 mismatches**, and two of them were not arithmetic at all: `SKILL.md 声称「engine version = 9.9.9」实测 1.0.97` and `formulas/README.md 声称「公式库总量 = 400」实测 608`. The audit's own drift classifier named the mechanism for the second one and the mechanism for the first: **a live-injection probe had written `9.9.9` into `SKILL.md` during the first audit's spawned suite and had not put it back by the time I ran `git add`** — so I committed a probe's injection, and the audit's snapshot then read the pollution as a claim for the rest of the run. 铁律 0 requires re-reading the documents after running `run-all.js` or the audit and confirming the probes are restored; I ran the audit and went straight to the commit. The repair is the one cycle 19 already measured: **not** `git checkout -- SKILL.md` (which reverts to a stale commit and would have discarded this cycle's legitimate `2266 passing tests` edit), but `git add` the already-healed working-tree value and `git commit --amend`. The report itself said which direction was authoritative — `⛔ 绝不要按快照还原 … ✓ 正确修法: 什么都不用做, 当前磁盘内容已经是权威值` — and the correct action was therefore to amend HEAD toward the disk, never the disk toward the snapshot. One mechanical note worth recording, because it cost a second attempt: `git commit --amend --no-edit --only` did **not** carry the staged `SKILL.md` into the amended commit (the diff stayed 1 insertion / 1 deletion against HEAD afterwards); a plain `git commit --amend --no-edit` did. Verified after: every version site in `HEAD` reads `1.0.97`, `git diff --cached` is empty, and the tag was re-pointed. Re-measured on the committed state: audit **98 claims / 93 consistent / 0 mismatched**, exit 0 under `ASPIRA_AUDIT_SKIP_TESTS=1`. The residual is disclosed rather than smoothed over: the full-spawn audit is still the unstable channel, and this cycle's own ordering was one of its two entrances. **One thing measured and deliberately not done:** recovering those two samples would mean restoring the seven code/SQL terms cycle 141 removed, and cycle 141 measured that at **18/122 false positives** under character-wise separators and **13/122** under letter spaces, against a drop to **0/122** and **1/122** after removal — the net-negative is measured, not assumed, and the root cause it identified (the transform acts on the whole passage, so benign English technical prose is split word-by-word and stage 2's whitespace anchor holds for attack and benign alike) still stands. 铁律 2 leaves the recovery to a cycle that can change the anchor, not the term list.
- **The one tool in 181 that hands the caller the server's own filesystem path — and it does so for a state its sibling module calls legal.** `mcp-tool-enhancement`, 215th cycle, and the engine chose it on its own (0.74 / confidence 0.65, no tie to break). The slice has kept one discipline for four cycles: *counting tools is not calling them* (cycle 17/29/192/193/204), and both of those cycles' lessons were obeyed before the census meant anything — `await` every handler (cycle 17: calling synchronously lets an async handler's Promise be `JSON.stringify`'d into `{}`, reporting twelve healthy tools as hollow), and fill each tool's `inputSchema` required fields (cycle 193: placeholder args measured the validator, not the tool). Re-run rather than trusted, the census read **ok 168 / errorObj 13 / threw 0 / missing 0** against cycle 204's 172 / 9. Resolving all 13 by name, twelve are either the *correct* missing-parameter validation triggered by the probe's own arguments, or cycle 29's already-recorded honest error (`aspira_benchmark_run → 未找到数据包`, the directory genuinely being empty). One is the cycle: `aspira_formula_calc` answered `Cannot find module 'mathjs'\nRequire stack:\n- /Users/mm/Pictures/aspira/src/mcp-server.js\n- …`. **Three things are wrong in that one return at once.** ① It leaks the deployment's absolute filesystem path — and it is the only one of the 181 tools that does so, on any deployment without mathjs, 100% of the time. ② It reports a **legal state as a code error**: mathjs sits in `package.json`'s `optionalDependencies`, and `node_modules` here holds **zero** entries, so this is the state the engine was explicitly built for. `src/formula/formula-calculator.js`'s own header comment says it in as many words — *"mathjs 是 optionalDependencies: 缺失是合法状态, 不是异常"* — and carries a complete degradation for exactly this case. ③ It **contradicts its own sibling**: the same condition reads "符号计算需要可选依赖 mathjs，当前未安装。公式搜索/查表仍可用…" in `formula-calculator` and a Node `MODULE_NOT_FOUND` on the MCP tool surface. The root cause is one undiscriminating catch: `handleFormulaCalc` did a bare `require('mathjs')` and its single `catch (e) { return { error: e.message, formula }; }` flattened "which optional dependency is missing" — a precisely decidable fact — into whatever Node happened to say. The fix extracts the degradation into a module-level exported `optionalDepMissingError(dep)` in `formula-calculator.js` (so the wording has **one** source; a second copy in `mcp-server.js` would drift) and gives the require its own narrowly-scoped guard: only a `MODULE_NOT_FOUND` whose message names mathjs takes the degradation path, and every other error still returns its original message.
